> Update this file after every meaningful implementation change. This is the one living "where we are" doc â€” it replaces the scattered point-in-time reports that used to live under `docs/archive/` (deleted in the 2026-06-21 cleanup pass; recoverable from git history if ever needed).

## Current Phase

**Pre-release hardening.** The platform is built and integrated (Stripe, Checkr, Linq messaging, Twilio Video, Firebase, Evia). Focus is now correctness, single-source-of-truth, and closing the gap between what the docs claim and what runs end-to-end.

## Current Goal

Adopt the spec-driven workflow (these `context/` files), make type-safety actually enforced, and verify the release-gating success criteria in [project-overview.md](./project-overview.md).

LOG FINDING: every caregiver text after the START re-subscribe (18:12:55–18:17:45Z) produced a Linq send from Evia, yet none reached the phone — the chat was very likely opted out at Linq/carrier level by END and our START only cleared OUR flag (optInPhoneNumber updates the session doc only); the founder ended the visit from the site at 11:18. Founder confirmed 9/29 that Evia texts reach that number again — the hold cleared on its own (carrier-side START processing lags); no provider action needed. The 5 replies sent during the hold were lost. Login is Firebase phone auth (Google's SMS sender) — an Evia opt-out never blocks the login code; the only Evia-sent code is accountRecovery's phone-change OTP, which goes to the NEW number — when that number has opted out, the web step now says "Text START to Evia from that phone, then try again — or change your number from Account Settings" instead of the raw send error. Post-shift (timesheet) is the next page, per founder.

(2) `matchDecisionWord` is exact-word only: a bare yes/no/ok never maps to ACCEPT / DECLINE any more (only the decline confirm offers YES / NO) and never reaches the classifier while another kind is parked — the agent may have asked its own yes/no question. Kinds: interview_request (ACCEPT gated like the page → respondToInterviewRequest, "Interview accepted"/"Interview declined"; PROPOSE → the reschedule flow), interview_proposal (either side: CONFIRM → accept_interview_reschedule, "Interview time confirmed"; PROPOSE → caregiver reschedule flow / family asked for a time and left to the agent), booking_request (ACCEPT gated → the page's accept + toast; DECLINE → the page's confirm "Decline this booking request? Wired in `routeCaregiver.ts` (after the scripted flows, before keywords) and `routeClient.ts` (after every family flow, before the agent). Notices now say the exact words: interview request "Reply ACCEPT or DECLINE, or PROPOSE a different time.", proposals "Reply CONFIRM, or suggest another time." / "Reply CONFIRM, or PROPOSE another time.", new job "Reply APPLY or DETAILS." **Booking-request and schedule-change notices carry the tab's card WHOLE** (founder: "why didn't I get the full details of the booking") — the site's lead sentence, then the card + full details (recipient notes included), then "Reply ACCEPT or DECLINE." — no DETAILS step (DETAILS still accepted as a word). Founder also reported the recipient note missing on the live card: the render IS in the live CaregiverBookingsPage chunk (BfUzxMtr) — a cached pre-deploy chunk; hard refresh. No extra "are you sure?" on Accept (the page has none). Latest notice wins; every toast names the outcome. Root causes: (1) the frequency answer "Occasional" was absorbed by absorbClientFields as the care need "occasional help" → careNeeds counted as answered → the "what kind of help" question was never asked, the summary read "(occasional help)" and the Care Plan showed it as a pill. Directive: every STILL NEEDED / ALREADY KNOWN line now leads with the save key ('smokingHousehold — whether anyone…`), explicit rules for the two pets/smoking saves + jobDescription, "never decline an item on privacy grounds / no other client is involved", careNeeds ≠ careFrequency, and the emergencyContactRelationship label now says it is the family's own contact. (2) `requestEmailChangeSelf` (site Account Settings + Evia `request_email_change`): if the current address is confirmed → approval link to the OLD address (`/approve-email-change`, new `ApproveEmailChangePage`) + Evia text "reply APPROVE to use this phone as proof, or NO" (`agents/emailChangeReply.ts` anchor in routeIntent); site fallback "Text me a code" ('start_email_change_fallback` / `confirm_email_change_fallback`); only after approval does the new inbox get its link (`/verify-email-change`), and `confirmEmailChange` swaps + sets `emailVerified/emailVerifiedAt/emailVerifiedFor`, texts, bells (`account_email_confirmed` → Account Settings), and emails the old address a notice. Unconfirmed current address → new inbox's link straight away (nothing to anchor to). (3) Phone change BLOCKED until the email is confirmed: `requestPhoneChangeByEmail` gate (silent, anti-enumeration), both `update_user_profile` requestPhoneChange branches return `emailUnverified`, both settings pages show "Confirm your recovery email first" + Resend (`resend_email_confirmation`), Evia's ACCOUNT STATUS line + `get_account_settings` (`recoveryEmailVerified`, resend action) say the same. New action types + server-only fields added to firestore.rules (deploy rules!). - **2026-09-15 (My Bookings parity, round 2 — reschedule + skip as scripted flows, memory-detector hijack fixed, recipient-roster safeguards; typecheck clean, full functions suite green).** Driven by a second live SMS session (12:40 AM, logs pulled): "no skip" → Evia said "Okay, skipping that" with NO write; "9/17 10am to 3pm" → `propose_reschedule` on the WRONG visit (the needs_replacement one, toolError); "skip the shift for tomorrow. no need for replacement" → swallowed by the memory-correction detector (`factChangeOutcome: no_match`). Founder's directive: Evia checks live data (the family can change bookings on the site at any moment) and actually writes.
  - **New scripted `rescheduleFlow.ts`** + entry tool 'start_reschedule_flow` + client `RESCHEDULE_REQUEST` routed straight into it (no agent turn): the site's Reschedule button on an upcoming shift, step for step — the family's REAL 'scheduled` visits read fresh from 'shifts` → their own words parsed against that list (never a shiftId the model guessed) → pick only if ambiguous → new day + start/end (time-only keeps the day; past dates refused) → recap → YES → the same `reschedulePending*`/`rescheduledBy:"client"` write. Commit re-loads the visit (refuses if it changed on the site since) and runs the site's own same-caregiver overlap check. Registered in all four 'sessionState.ts` registries, `routeClient.ts` pre-intent dispatch, `toolCapabilities.ts`. 18 tests (`rescheduleFlow.test.ts`).
  - **New shared `agents/shiftReschedule.ts`** (`listReschedulableVisits`, `loadReschedulableShift`, `findOwnShiftConflict` = the site's `fetchOwnShiftsForDate`+`rangeConflicts`, `validateRescheduleTarget`, `proposeShiftReschedule`) — `manage_booking propose_reschedule` now goes through it too, so the tool gained the conflict check it never had (parity gap closed) and can't drift from the flow.
  - **Skip is now a real flow step** (`replacementFlow.ts` `rp_skip_confirm`, shared 'skipReplacementShift` = `cancel_visit`'s write): offered by name next to the candidates (the site's two buttons), reachable by bare SKIP / model-parsed skip at pick or recap, and the ONLY path when no candidates are found (previously a dead-end message with no state). YES → 'status:"cancelled", cancelledBy:"client"` on that shift only; NO → back to candidates / left as Needs Replacement. 5 tests.
  - **New `FIND_REPLACEMENT` intent** (classifier + capabilities + shadow-tap): exactly one `needs_replacement` visit → 'startReplacementFlow` directly (the live "who is available for replacement" → general search detour is closed at routing, not just in the prompt); zero/several → the agent.
  - **Memory-correction detector no longer hijacks booking actions** (`qaAgent.ts`): skipped entirely when the intent classifier already judged the turn an action (`FACT_CHANGE_SKIP_INTENTS`: reschedule/cancel/find/hire/swap/…); a `no_match` verdict only blocks the turn when the classifier independently said `FACT_CORRECTION` — otherwise the agent (which sees live data) gets the turn. Prompt: new knowledge-boundary rule (never assert a visit exists/doesn't/its status from memory or the snapshot — `get_upcoming_appointments` THIS turn, match by id+date; never claim cancelled/skipped/moved/sent without a tool success), RESCHEDULING → 'start_reschedule_flow`, cancel_visit = the site's Skip with read-back + YES first. 3 tests.

Deleting from the Care Plan itself still removes a recipient.

  - **New scripted `replacementFlow.ts`** + entry tool 'start_replacement_flow`: the site's Find Replacement modal step for step (candidate profile cards → which one + keep/change the visit's date/time → recap → YES → the same `booking_requests` write the modal's Request button makes; back-out on every step; nothing written before YES). Shares one implementation with `get_callout_backups`/'select_callout_backup` via new helpers in `agents/shiftReplacement.ts` (`loadReplacementShift`, 'sendReplacementCandidateCards`, `createReplacementRequest`, `describeVisitWindow`). Wired into `routeClient.ts` pre-intent dispatch + 'sessionState.ts` registries. 11 tests.

New `get_pending_booking_requests` (client + caregiver) = the Requests tab. Client notification clicks for booking/shift/amendment types now route to `/client/bookings` (`NotificationDropdown.tsx`).

- **2026-09-13 (new scripted `interviewFlow.ts` + real mid-flow back-off rolled out to all 3 major scripted flows — typecheck clean, full functions suite green (312/312 files, 3921/3929 tests, 8 pre-existing skips), COMMITTED/PUSHED/DEPLOYED).** Live SMS test found 'schedule_interview` had no scripted flow at all (unlike booking/job-posting) — a legitimate mid-flow question ("can you link to a job post") got misclassified by intent-routing as `POST_JOB` and hijacked the entire session into `jobPostingFlow.ts`, which (same gap, see below) had no way back out either — the family was stuck re-answering an unrelated frequency question no matter what they said, including direct answers to Evia's own clarifying question.
  - **`functions/src/agents/interviewFlow.ts`** (new) — scripted step machine for interview scheduling, matching the site's real "Request Interview" modal section order: Related Job Post (only asked when the client has open posts; auto-skipped and pre-filled when `applicationId` is already known) → Date → Time → optional Notes → full recap → YES/edit/cancel. No interview-type question at all — confirmed the site removed Phone/In-Person entirely on 2026-09-08, every interview is video. Commits directly via `requestVideoInterview` (`videoInterviewRequest.ts`), same direct-call pattern `bookingFlow.ts` uses for `createBookingTask` — no MCP tool, no pending-action gate, since the flow owns its own confirm step.
  - **New MCP tool 'start_interview_flow`** — the preferred entry point now (mirrors 'start_booking_flow`); 'schedule_interview` demoted to "low-level, only for cases already resolved outside the flow" (mirrors `request_booking`'s demotion). Wired into `routeClient.ts`'s pre-intent dispatch (the actual derailment fix — captures every reply deterministically before intent classification runs), plus 'sessionState.ts`/`webhooks.ts`/`utils/language.ts`/`toolCapabilities.ts`/`qaAgent.ts` prompt (the specific lines that told the agent to call 'schedule_interview` directly in common scenarios now point to 'start_interview_flow`).
  - **New shared `isBackOutRequest` classifier** ('stepHandler.ts`, the canonical shared-guard framework) — distinguishes a genuine "never mind/cancel this/stop" from an off-topic question (isQuestionOrOther's job) or an in-flow edit request. `interviewFlow.ts` was built with this checked FIRST in every step from the start.
  - **Retrofitted onto `bookingFlow.ts` and `jobPostingFlow.ts`**, which previously only recognized a cancel at their own FINAL confirm step — anywhere earlier, "never mind" fell through as an off-topic aside and just re-asked the same question forever (confirmed this is exactly what trapped the family in the live incident above, even after "cancel this request" hit `jobPostingFlow.ts`'s own generated clarifying question). Added to all 8 `bookingFlow.ts` steps (refactoring its existing confirm-step cancel branch to reuse the same new shared helper) and all 13 `jobPostingFlow.ts` steps.
  - **41 new tests** across `interviewFlow.test.ts` (18), `bookingBackOut.test.ts` (9), `jobPostingBackOut.test.ts` (14) — covering every step's back-out, that a genuine answer still proceeds normally, and (for the new flow) every step/edit/error path. All pre-existing tests for the two retrofitted flows (30 + 21) still pass unchanged.
  - **Remaining queue** (not started): the same early-exit capability for `clientSwapRequestHandler.ts`, `availabilityHandler.ts`, `caregiverProfileHandler.ts` (6 sub-flows, one shared fix), `caregiverCancelShiftHandler.ts` (has the inverse gap — cancel mid-flow but not at its last step), `taskApprovalHandler.ts` (different shape, needs bespoke wiring). `timesheetHandler.ts` still needs a decision on whether "back off" even applies (payroll adjudication may need to force a real decision). `refundHandler.ts` is excluded — that flow is being removed entirely (see memory).
  - **Not yet live-tested** — no Firestore/SMS access in this sandbox; the user will retest the exact "link this interview to a job post" / mid-flow cancel scenarios that motivated this work.

- **2026-09-13 (two more small live-caught bugs, found while testing the schedule_interview job-post pick and the interview fit-decision path — typecheck clean, full functions suite green (309/309 files, 3880/3888 tests), COMMITTED/PUSHED/DEPLOYED).**
  - **`list_client_jobs`** was computing its own generic title ('summary` or a `careTypes` join) instead of reading the job post's real, required `title` field — the same one the site's own dropdowns/lists display ('services/api.ts`'s `normalizeJobPost`). This surfaced immediately: the new 'schedule_interview` job-post pick read out generic labels ("light housekeeping," "companionship") instead of the site's actual post titles + dates. Fixed to prefer `data.title` first, matching the site's own fallback chain.
  - **'submit_interview_feedback`** permanently locked an interview out of ever recording a real hire/no decision once "maybe" was given — `feedbackSubmitted: true` was stamped for all three fit levels alike, and the tool's own guard rejected any later call with "Feedback already submitted," even though `interviewFeedbackNudge.ts` (fixed earlier the same day) deliberately keeps re-asking after "maybe" because it isn't a final answer. The nudge could re-ask forever; the tool could never actually record the eventual real answer. Fixed the guard to only block when the interview's current `fitLevel` is already terminal ('strong`/`no`), matching the nudge's own terminal check.
  - **User decision, explicitly deferred (not a bug — a scoped-out gap):** the interview-completion nudge's per-attempt "wait tool" LLM judgment ('shouldSend` in `caraAgent.ts`) held back the same nudge 7 times in a row for a live test interview (confirmed via direct Firestore/log inspection: cron healthy, daily cap never even reached, `reason: "wait_tool"` on every attempt) — user's call: **"keep it the way it was."** No code changed for this.
  - **User decision, explicitly deferred (not a bug — a scoped-out gap):** `manage_booking`'s `resend_booking` action is a blind resend (flips `booking_requests.status` back to `pending`, same rate/schedule/notes, zero review-and-edit step) — unlike the site's own "Resend" button, which reopens the identical editable "Send Booking Request" modal used for a fresh send. User's call: **"we will keep the same function for the resend."** No code changed for this.
  - **New, unbuilt gap surfaced by discussion (flagged, not yet started):** there is no tool that lists a caregiver's past declined/cancelled `booking_requests` docs, so `resend_booking` requires a `bookingRequestId` Evia has no way to look up if it isn't already in conversation context — with 2+ past declined bookings (same or different caregivers), nothing lets the family pick "which one" the way the job-post numbered-list pick now works for 'schedule_interview`. Similarly, 'start_booking_flow`'s optional `interviewId` (for job-post/rate linkage) has no forced disambiguation step when a caregiver has multiple interviews on file (exactly [[project_interview_testing_continuation]]'s live 3-interviews-same-caregiver scenario) — the agent can call `list_interviews` to check, but nothing requires it to actually surface the choice to the family first. **Queued for next session, after the user's usage limit resets.**

- **2026-09-13 (booking flow deepened to full site data-parity + a real site bug found and fixed in the live "Send Booking Request" modal + schedule_interview's job-post pick — typecheck clean, full functions suite green (309/309 files, 3877/3886 tests, 1 flaky timeout reconfirmed passing standalone), COMMITTED/PUSHED/DEPLOYED).** Follow-on session to the `56bde06`/`ca68373` scripted-flow work below, driven entirely by live comparison against the real site modal/Care Plan page:
  - **Recipient defaulting fixed**: a 2+-recipient household with none named used to default to the primary recipient only (`recipientResolved: "defaulted_primary"`); now defaults to **every** recipient, matching the site's own pre-selected-all checkbox behavior (`recipientResolved: "defaulted_all"`). Single-recipient households now also populate the full `careRecipients` array (not just `recipientName`/`recipientKey`), so the recap/edit code has one consistent shape regardless of household size.
  - **Rate and days are now ALWAYS asked and confirmed**, never silently pre-filled from a linked job post and skipped — `RATE_QUESTION`/`DAYS_QUESTION` mention the job post's number as a suggestion but still require an explicit reply, renamed "Rate" → "**Agreed rate**" in the recap to match the site's own label.
  - **Per-day start/end times are now required for every day** in a recurring schedule (`dayTimes: Record<string, {start,end}>`), replacing one shared start/end applied across all days — re-asks if any day is missing or invalid.
  - **Care needs, lifestyle, and notes are now all visible and editable** in the recap, not just shown as "not specified": `formatRecipientLifestyle()` renders the full structured lifestyle object (favorite activities, entertainment, conversation/quiet/family/friends/appointments), a `Notes:` line renders each recipient's Care Plan notes field, and three new `bk_confirm` edit actions (`edit_care_needs`, `edit_lifestyle`, `edit_notes`) let the family correct any of them free-text (extracted via a second `parseWithClaude` call, merged/replaced into the right recipient — disambiguating by name when there's more than one — before re-showing the recap). `edit_notes` **overwrites** (matches the site's plain single textarea), while care-needs/lifestyle **merge**.
  - **"Message to {caregiver}" is now proactively asked once**, right before the recap (mirrors the modal's own field position just above Send), instead of only being editable after the fact — classifies SKIP vs. an actual note via `parseWithClaude` since the same question is now reached both proactively and via a later edit.
  - **Care recipient selection is now itself editable** (`edit_recipients` action + `promptRecipientEdit`/`applyRecipientSelection`/`listRecipientOptions`), matching the site's own select/deselect-recipients toggle in Edit mode.
  - **Site bug found and fixed** (not an Evia gap — the real "Send Booking Request" modal itself): `components/client/PostsPage.tsx`'s Care Plan Details section rendered Care Needs and Lifestyle & Preferences per recipient but never the recipient's own Care Plan **Notes** field, even though the note was already being silently included in the submitted booking payload — and any edit made to a note inside the modal was discarded on submit (the code always re-read the saved plan's note, never the draft). Fixed: a new Notes block renders between Care Needs and Lifestyle & Preferences (matching `CarePlan.tsx`'s own section order), read-only text normally / an editable textarea in Edit mode, and the submit payload now prefers the edited draft over the saved value.
  - **'schedule_interview`'s "Related Job Post" pick** (the site's modal has a dropdown for this, defaulting to "No specific post"): Evia's tool description previously grouped it with interview-type/notes as "mention only if it fits naturally, never ask." Split out and strengthened — unless the interview is already coming from accepting a job application (which auto-links its own `jobId`), the agent is now told to call `list_client_jobs` first and, if the family has any open posts, actively offer them as a short numbered list before scheduling. Still fully optional. Matters because `jobId` is what `resolveInterviewLinkage` (`bookingResolution.ts`) later uses to pull `jobTitle`/`jobPostRate`/`jobPostSchedule` into a booking — skipping it loses that traceability even though the booking flow's own always-ask rate/days keeps the booking itself unblocked. Prompt-only change (no scripted flow exists for interview scheduling yet) — worth confirming live that the model actually follows it consistently.
  - **Not yet live-tested** — no Firestore/SMS access in this sandbox; the user plans to test after this deploys.

- **2026-09-13 (scripted booking flow — matches the site's "Send Booking Request" review modal — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (309/309 files, 3863/3871 tests, 8 pre-existing skips), COMMITTED/PUSHED/DEPLOYED — commits `56bde06` + `ca68373`, verified via `npm run deploy`'s own "Deploy complete!" both times).** Two follow-up fixes added in the same session, both prompted by the user's own questions before deploy: (1) the confirm recap was YES/NO only — added edit support (`handleBkConfirm` now classifies confirm/cancel/edit_rate/edit_schedule/edit_location/other; an in-message correction like "actually make it $28/hr" applies in place and re-shows the recap, a vague edit request jumps back to that field's question, `edit_location` always re-lists real saved addresses via a new `listCareLocationOptions` in `bookingResolution.ts` rather than silently re-picking the same one); (2) the recurring-schedule branch had `ongoing: true` hardcoded with no way to set a fixed end date — added a `bk_ask_ongoing` step (skipped when a linked job post already has a real end date on file) so a family can say "ongoing" or "through December 1" and have it actually carried through. `bookingFlow.test.ts` grew from 11 → 17 tests to cover both. **Not yet live-tested** — no Firestore/SMS access in this sandbox. Live SMS test (same session, after the recurring-schedule reconcile work below) sending a booking for a completed interview stalled: after the family answered the ambiguous-care-location question, Evia's next reply tripped the grounding-classifier handoff ("I'm looping in a teammate..."), and every follow-up ("are you there") then got misclassified as a memory-correction request (`FACT_CHANGE_NO_MATCH_COPY`), permanently derailing the conversation. Root cause: `request_booking` was never a scripted flow — just an MCP tool the general qaAgent loop called ad hoc, with no structured recap (its own confirm-gate preview is a generic one-line dash-joined string, not the site's actual review layout) and no protection from an in-progress reply being hijacked by intent classification.
  Compared directly against the site's real "Send Booking Request" modal (`components/client/PostsPage.tsx:1665-2561`, `handleSendBooking`): it's a REVIEW screen, not a wizard — pre-filled except two "Required" fields (Rate & Payment, Care Location), with Schedule/Care Recipients/Care Plan Details/Lifestyle/Emergency Contact shown read-only. User confirmed (via plan-mode sign-off) the new flow's ask/show order must match this exact section order.
  Built, mirroring `jobPostingFlow.ts`'s established pattern (session-state field machine on `agent_sessions/{phone}.bookingFlowStep`/`.bookingFlowData`, one question per turn, `isQuestionOrOther` guard, re-ask-never-silently-default on failed extraction, ending in its own structured recap + explicit YES/NO — no MCP tool, no pending-action gate involved, since tracing the confirm-gate pipeline (`pendingActions.ts`/`approvalHandler.ts`/`webhooks.ts:2889-2921`) confirmed a family's plain "YES" is intercepted globally BEFORE any scripted flow's step code runs, so reusing `request_booking`'s existing gate was architecturally not viable — every other scripted flow in this codebase implements its own gate and calls the real work function directly, same pattern followed here):
  - **`functions/src/agents/bookingResolution.ts`** (new) — extracted ~450 lines of resolution logic that used to live inline in `mcp/server.ts`'s `request_booking` case (interview/job linkage, rate resolution, care-location resolution + ambiguity listing, recipient attribution, emergency contact lookup, top-level care-needs/lifestyle derivation, per-recipient age/relationship enrichment) into small, independently callable, reusable functions — one home instead of duplicating it between the MCP tool and the new flow. `request_booking`'s case in `mcp/server.ts` refactored to call these with **zero intended behavior change** (verified: `booking.test.ts`/`manageBooking.test.ts`/`coverage-smoke.test.ts` all green before writing a single new test).
  - **`functions/src/agents/bookingFlow.ts`** (new) — the scripted flow itself: `bk_ask_rate` (skipped if a linked job post already has a rate) → `bk_ask_days` (classifies recurring weekly days vs. a specific one-off date — skipped straight to times if the linked job post already lists days, matching `request_booking`'s own existing behavior) → `bk_ask_times` → `bk_ask_location` (only if 2+ saved addresses) → `bk_confirm` (full recap in modal order: Caregiver / Rate & Payment / Schedule / Care Recipients / Care Plan Details / Lifestyle & Preferences / Care Location / Emergency Contact — all the pre-filled sections computed once via the resolvers, never asked as their own question, matching the site never asking for them either). On YES, calls `createBookingTask` (`bookingExecutor.ts`) directly, mirroring `buildAndSaveJobPost`'s direct-call pattern.
  - **New MCP tool 'start_booking_flow`** (`caregiverId`, optional `interviewId`) — the new preferred agent-facing entry point once a family is ready to book (replacing the old prompt guidance that told the agent to collect fields itself and call `request_booking` directly); its handler just calls 'startBookingFlow(...)` and tells the agent not to send anything else that turn (same self-send convention as `resend_caregiver_profile`). `request_booking` itself is kept, now documented as "low-level, prefer start_booking_flow" — still used as-is by existing non-conversational callers (e.g. the rebook path in `routeIntent.ts`).
  - **Wiring**: `routeClient.ts` gained a `bookingFlowStep` pre-intent state-machine branch (same shape as the existing `jobPostingStep` one) — this is what actually fixes today's bug, since every reply is now captured deterministically before intent classification (and its FACT_CORRECTION misfire) ever gets a chance to run. 'sessionState.ts` registries (`STATE_MACHINE_FLAGS`, `GUARDED_SMS_FLAGS`, `PASSIVE_SMS_FLAGS`, `RESUMABLE_FLOW_DESCRIPTIONS`), `webhooks.ts`'s timeout-flow-key detection, and `utils/language.ts`'s `flowLabel` map all got matching `bookingFlowStep`/`booking` entries. `qaAgent.ts`'s prompt guidance for `request_booking`/'submit_interview_feedback` rewritten to point at 'start_booking_flow`.
  - **Deliberately requires a real starting schedule** (days/times), unlike the site, which can send a booking with schedule completely unset ("Ongoing" badge, "No schedule set"). Traced why: `bookingExecutor.ts`'s `executeBookings` (the caregiver-notification path) crashes today at `task.appointments[0].durationHours` when appointments is empty — it always builds a shift-specific SMS offer, unlike the site's caregiver Accept button which needs no shift reference at all. User explicitly chose (option presented, not assumed) to always collect a starting schedule for v1 rather than also fixing that caregiver-side crash risk; a fully schedule-less "Ongoing" booking stays a known, flagged gap for a later pass.
  - **New tests**: `bookingResolution.test.ts` (18 tests — rate fallback order, care-location single/ambiguous/fallback/refuse, interview linkage, recipient attribution incl. multi-recipient, emergency contact, the moved `bookingTimeToMinutes`/`parseBookingDateRange` helpers) and `bookingFlow.test.ts` (11 tests — each step's skip-when-resolvable and re-ask-on-failure behavior, the recurring/one-off branch, mid-flow question handling, the full YES/NO confirm commit path incl. the exact `createBookingTask` call shape, and the recap's section ordering).

The `modifyScheduleFlow.ts` SMS conversational flow — a second door into the same dead model, reachable via the `MODIFY_SCHEDULE` intent in `routeIntent.ts` and a `modifyScheduleStep` session flag in `routeClient.ts`, bypassing the MCP tool catalog entirely (same "second door" shape as the healthcareHandler.ts/schedulingHandler.ts removals from 2026-09-05) — was deleted outright along with its routing hooks and `modifyScheduleStep`/`modifyScheduleData` session-flag registrations in 'sessionState.ts` (`STATE_MACHINE_FLAGS`, `GUARDED_SMS_FLAGS`, `PASSIVE_SMS_FLAGS`, `RESUMABLE_FLOW_DESCRIPTIONS`) and `webhooks.ts`. `AGENT_NATIVE_EXCLUSIONS.md`'s U15 punch-list row updated to record the removal rationale instead of the stale "kept as one action-discriminated primitive" note.

- **2026-09-09 (full removal of the `interview_requests` collection and its entire legacy negotiation flow — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean (frontend + functions), full functions suite green (302/302 files, 3676/3684 tests) plus the root-level suite (`npm test -- --run`, 325/325 files, 4054/4062 tests — one unrelated flaky timeout on `pricingLiterals.test.ts` confirmed to pass in isolation), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Follow-up #23 (2026-09-07, below) only removed the ENTRY POINT into `interviewAgent.ts`'s caregiver-negotiates-first flow, deliberately leaving the rest of that chain (`handleInterviewSelection`, `handleCaregiverAvailabilityReply`, `handleInterviewConfirm`, 'sendPostInterviewFollowUp`, `writeInterviewOutcomeSignal`) alive in case an in-flight negotiation needed to finish. Two genuinely live-but-broken call sites needed care rather than deletion: `routeCaregiver.ts`'s unconditional `PASS` keyword handler and its `caregiverRescheduling` block both used to query the now-dead `interview_requests` collection and would always find nothing — inlined the exact acknowledgment message each already fell through to regardless, so observable caregiver-facing behavior is bit-for-bit unchanged, just without the wasted dead query. Also simplified `matchingAgent.ts`'s `buildFamilyMatchHistory` (the interview_requests half was frozen historical data at best, since nothing writes it anymore) and removed the now-fully-unused `pendingTimeSelection`/`pendingInterviewConfirm`/`pendingInterviewOutcome` flags from every 'sessionState.ts` registry (`STATE_MACHINE_FLAGS`, `HIGH_STAKES_CONFIRM_FLAGS`, `GUARDED_SMS_FLAGS`, `PASSIVE_SMS_FLAGS`, `RESUMABLE_FLOW_DESCRIPTIONS`). Updated the 8 test files that mocked/imported the removed module or asserted on the removed flags/functions.

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #26, same session: jobPostingFlow.ts — the SMS CONVERSATION behind "post a new job" (distinct from the `create_job_post` MCP tool fixed in follow-up #24) — brought up to full parity with the site's 6-step wizard, plus a live-caught duplicate-confirmation bug in the interview flow — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (302/302 files, 3647/3655 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse ran a real end-to-end test of the SMS job-posting conversation and it fell apart in several distinct ways at once: "next Monday" resolved to a date over a year in the past; every correction attempt ("I said 9/14", "i said Saturday" ×2, "i didn't say companionship") was silently steamrolled by a fabricated default value instead of being recognized as a correction; a plain "24" for hourly rate became "flexible"; the mid-conversation recap and the final confirm summary disagreed with each other; and the user's own frustrated correction text ended up embedded verbatim in the job's public description field.
  Root cause, found directly in code: every one of the file's 9 step handlers followed the same pattern — extract with a raw direct `getSharedClient().messages.create()` call (bypassing the project's shared `parseWithClaude`/`quickComplete` helper), and on ANY unrecognized or failed extraction, silently substitute a hardcoded default (`["Monday","Wednesday","Friday"]` for days, `["morning"]` for time, `["Companionship"]` for care needs, `"moderate"` for care level, rate defaulting to flexible on any non-numeric parse) and announce that default as if it were the user's real, confirmed answer — with no re-ask, no signal anything went wrong. A correction attempt is neither a recognized question (so the `isQuestionOrOther` guard doesn't catch it) nor a valid answer to the current question, so it fell straight through into this same defaulting path every time. Separately, the start-date extraction prompt never told the model what today's actual date was, so any relative date ("next Monday") was pure guesswork.
  Fix: every step now re-asks (with a distinct "Sorry, I didn't quite catch that" prefix) instead of substituting a fabricated default when a parse fails or comes back unrecognized — nothing merges or advances until a real, recognized answer lands. The start-date prompt now includes the real current date and day-of-week (Pacific time). Also reordered the entire flow to match the site's actual step sequence — confirmed via `PostJobFlow.tsx`'s component order and the wizard's own "Step 2 of 6" label — after an intermediate self-caught mistake of building who/where BEFORE schedule instead of after: **frequency → start date → days → time → who's it for → how many caregivers → which address → care needs → hourly rate → job title → description → confirm**. `jobCareLevel` ("light/moderate/full care") is no longer its own question at all — grepped the site's own wizard and confirmed it has NO input control anywhere that ever sets this field (display-only on the final review screen) — now derived automatically from care needs via the same `deriveCareLevel` helper the onboarding job-posting path already uses, matching the site's real behavior of never asking.
  **Full who/where parity build** (the actual ask: "I want it to ask everything the site does and function the same as the site and be connected"): added three new conversational steps (`jp_ask_recipients`, `jp_ask_caregivers_needed`, `jp_ask_location`, plus two conditional sub-steps for a brand-new person's relationship or a brand-new address's pets/smoking) that read the SAME `job_postings/{uid}` + `carePlans/{uid}.locationPool` data the site's own Step2WhoWhere.tsx picker reads — existing recipients/addresses are shown as a numbered list to pick from, or the family can name someone/somewhere new. Rewired `buildJobPost.ts` (the finalization writer) to actually consume this per-job selection instead of only ever deriving from the stale onboarding-time snapshot: `caregiversNeeded` now actually reaches the public job listing (previously silently dropped); a genuinely new recipient or address gets appended to the account's persistent roster (`job_postings.additionalRecipients`/'savedLocations`, `carePlans.locationPool`) the same additive way the site's own `PostJobFlow.tsx` does (`arrayUnion`, "set primary only if none exists yet") — never overwritten/shrunk to match one job's narrower pick; `carePlans.locationPool` itself was previously overwritten down to a single entry on every job post (a pre-existing bug, fixed in the same pass since it directly undermines "pick from a previously-saved address" working at all).
  **Job title, added after Hamse flagged it directly** ("it should never be [auto-generated] care for the recipient, it has to match as the site"): confirmed via `Step5Describe.tsx` that the site auto-suggests "Senior care in {city}" but always lets the family type their own (10-80 chars) — `buildJobPost.ts` was instead silently manufacturing "Care for {name}"/"Light Care for {name}" with no way for the family to ever see or approve it. New `jp_ask_title` step (right after rate, matching the site's step position) offers the same default, accepts it verbatim or takes the family's own text, re-asks on an out-of-range length instead of guessing.
  **Explicitly NOT built, Hamse's call**: screening questions (site's optional, up-to-5, final wizard step) — verified by grep that this field is written by both the site and Evia's `create_job_post` tool but READ nowhere — no caregiver-facing component anywhere in the codebase displays or collects answers to it, on either channel. The wizard's own on-screen copy ("caregivers answer these when they apply") is currently aspirational, not functional. Left out of Evia's flow since building it would only add an equally-inert question, not real functionality.
  **Separately, live-caught and fixed in the same investigation**: a duplicate "your interview is in an hour" reminder (and duplicate confirmation texts) sent to a family, caught via a screenshot showing the identical message pair arriving together. Root cause: `handleInterviewConfirm` (`interviewAgent.ts`) minted a brand-new `video_interviews` doc and scheduled its own reminders every time it ran, and only cleared the session's `pendingInterviewConfirm` flag AFTER already sending texts — inbound SMS delivery is at-least-once, so the same "yes" redelivered by the carrier hit this unguarded window twice. Fixed by atomically claiming (read-and-clear, via a Firestore transaction) the confirmation flag before any work happens, so a redelivered "yes" for the same pending confirmation is now a silent no-op.
  Tests: `interviewConfirmDedup.test.ts` (new, 2 tests — single confirm still works, duplicate is blocked), `jobPostingWhoWhere.test.ts` (new, 13 tests covering every new who/where/title step and its re-ask behavior), `flowDispatch.test.ts` and `jobPostingFlow.test.ts` updated for the new field order and step count.

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #24, same session: create_job_post ("+ New Request" via Evia) brought up to full parity with the site's 6-step Post-a-Job wizard — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (300/300 files, 3630/3638 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Direct continuation of the interview-flow parity work — while confirming Evia's job-posting used the same shared writer functions as the site (`buildWebJobPostDoc`/`mirrorJobPostRecipientsToWeb`, true — no second competing system exists), a full field-by-field cross-check against the site's actual `JobPostFormData` type (not just the visible UI) turned up real gaps: `create_job_post` had no way to collect a `title` or `notes`/description at all (the site requires both — title 10-80 chars, description 50-2500 chars) — every SMS-posted job got an auto-generated generic title and zero description, `hourlyRate` was hard-required with no way to represent a flexible/negotiable rate (the site's "rate flexible" toggle), no way to pass sub-task detail (`careNeedDetails`, e.g. "Ambulation" under "Mobility Assistance"), and the `careRecipients` field was scoped to "only if not already on file" — meaning an EXISTING recipient's care plan (Notes, care needs) never got synced from a job post at all unless a brand-new recipient happened to be named in the same call.
  Fixed all of these: added required `title` (10-80 chars, server-validated) and `notes` (50-2500 chars, server-validated) parameters, wired through to both `buildWebJobPostDoc` (public job post) and `mirrorJobPostRecipientsToWeb` (care plan sync); made `hourlyRate` genuinely optional (`buildWebJobPostDoc` already auto-derives `rateFlexible` from a missing/zero rate — the gate was purely in `create_job_post`'s own validation); added `careNeedDetails` passthrough; changed `careRecipients`' guidance from "only new" to "every recipient this job covers, new or existing" — `mirrorJobPostRecipientsToWeb` already safely no-ops a roster duplicate for an existing primary recipient while still syncing their care plan, so this was a description/prompt fix, not a logic change. 6 new tests in `createJobPost.test.ts` (title/notes required, flexible-rate omission, careNeedDetails passthrough, existing-recipient care-plan sync without a roster duplicate) plus all 8 pre-existing tests updated for the new required fields.
  **Follow-up correction (same exchange)**: initially deferred `caregiversNeeded`/`dayShiftTimes`/`neighborhood` together as "all need a shared-contract change." Hamse pushed back with the site's actual screens, prompting a direct code check: only `caregiversNeeded` has any real, live UI control anywhere on the site (`Step2WhoWhere.tsx`'s "How many caregivers do you need?" stepper, confirmed in the screenshot). `dayShiftTimes` and `neighborhood` exist only in the site's own `JobPostFormData` TypeScript type — grepped both wizard step components and neither field has a single rendered control anywhere; the site itself never collects them either, so there was nothing live to actually match. Built `caregiversNeeded`: added to the shared `WebJobPostInput`/`buildWebJobPostDoc` contract (always set, defaults to 1, clamped 1-4 — matches `PostJobFlow.tsx`'s own write exactly), added the parameter to `create_job_post`'s schema, wired through the handler. 2 more tests (defaults to 1, clamps out-of-range input to 4). `dayShiftTimes`/`neighborhood` correctly NOT built — confirmed dead fields on the site's own side, not a real parity gap.

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #23, same session: removed the interview-scheduling flow with no website equivalent — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (300/300 files, 3625/3633 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** While explaining "what flow does Evia follow when scheduling an interview" to Hamse, traced that TWO genuinely different, both-live systems existed: (1) 'schedule_interview` → the shared `requestVideoInterview()` — the same function the website's own "Request Interview" modal submits to, same fields, same bookability re-check, same daily rate limit; (2) `interviewAgent.ts`'s `handleInterviewSelection` chain — triggered specifically by a bare-number reply ("2") within 2 hours of a match presentation — which asks the CAREGIVER for their availability first, cross-checks against the family's calendar, then confirms with the family, writing `video_interviews` directly with its own Meet-link call, completely bypassing the shared function and its protections. Confirmed via `git log`/`git show` that this second flow was NOT part of the Sep-5 "remove capabilities with no site equivalent" cleanup (`a6e4a62`) — that commit's real scope was 23 MCP tools plus two named "second door" handlers ('schedulingHandler.ts`, `healthcareHandler.ts`); `interviewAgent.ts` was actually touched in that SAME commit to fix an unrelated duplicate-request bug and gained new test coverage, confirming it was a deliberate keep, not an oversight — this genuinely fell outside that audit's scope. Hamse decided: remove it, keep only the site-matching flow.
  Fix, in `routeIntent.ts`: removed the interception that routed a bare-number match-list reply into `handleInterviewSelection`. `qaAgent.ts` already had purpose-built handling for exactly this case (a "CAREGIVERS YOU JUST SHOWED THIS FAMILY" context block instructing it to resolve a number/name/pronoun against `pendingMatches` and call 'schedule_interview`, asking for date/time first if needed) — it just never got the chance to run because the older flow claimed the reply first. A number reply now falls through to normal routing exactly like a name reply already did, so both converge on the one flow that matches the site. `handleInterviewSelection` itself was intentionally left in `interviewAgent.ts` (unreachable for new selections, but not deleted) so any `interview_requests` negotiation already in flight from before this ships can still finish normally rather than being stranded — did not touch the later stages of that chain (`handleCaregiverAvailabilityReply`, `handleInterviewConfirm`, the `pendingInterviewOutcome`/HIRE-MAYBE-PASS block) for the same reason; they naturally go dormant for new conversations once nothing creates new entries into them.
  2 new tests in `routeIntent.characterization.test.ts` confirming a bare-number reply no longer calls `handleInterviewSelection` and instead reaches `runQaAgent`, same as a name reply.

- **2026-09-07 — LIVE VALIDATION: the original decline-applicant bug (start of this session's investigation) is confirmed fully fixed end-to-end.** Hamse retested "let's decline her application" against the deployed code: real confirmation stub ("Just to be sure... This can't be undone."), "yes" → the genuine scripted "Done." success ack, the site's applicant count dropped 1→0, and the `job_applications` doc's 'status` field is now `"rejected"` in Firestore. This single action had failed three different ways earlier in this session (hallucinated completion with no tool call at all, then a confirmation-gate PERMISSION_DENIED, then a wrong-ID NOT_FOUND) — follow-ups #16 (confirmation-gate status bug) and #20 (wrong-ID guardrail) together are what made this specific round-trip succeed for real. Also separately observed and reported to Hamse, not yet acted on: two natural phrasings of "does this application have any notes" ("show application", "was there any notes added...") got deferred "I'm checking/I'm on it" non-answers with no tool call, while a third rephrasing ("who applied to the job") got an immediate, correct, tool-backed answer — likely the model treating a closely-related follow-up as "already in progress" once its own unfulfilled promise is in the conversation history, rather than triggering a fresh tool call. Flagged as a live example of the same "narrates instead of acting" class of issue this whole session has been chasing, this time as a silent omission rather than a false claim; no fix built yet, pending a decision on whether to pursue it.
  **Also live-validated the same session, right after**: follow-up #21's honest caregiver-search fallback. "do you have available caregivers" returned *"The closest matches near you are still Imran and Basra Yousuf, I'd already sent their info before. Want me to send their profiles again, or should I keep looking for someone new?"* — the exact new copy, word for word, replacing the old false "I don't have anyone available in your area." Note: Basra still surfacing here despite her job application having just been declined is expected/correct — declining an applicant to one job post and being excluded from general caregiver-search re-suggestion are two independent mechanisms (`job_applications.status` vs. `agent_sessions.shownCaregiverIds`), not a bug.

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #22, same session: Evia denied having a real cover letter on file — wrong field name — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (300/300 files, 3623/3631 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse asked Evia about Basra Yousuf's application; Evia said "no cover note and no proposed rate listed," then again "for any cover letters or additional notes, the Evia team... would be the best place to check" — while the website's own Applicants panel plainly showed a real cover-note quote ("I'm hard worker") right on the card, and the raw Firestore doc confirmed a `coverLetter: "i'm hard worker "` field.
  Root cause, found directly in code (no live-log guessing needed this time): `apply_to_job`'s handler writes BOTH `coverLetter` (the canonical field — what the website's own apply flow also writes) and `coverNote` (a compat alias, explicitly commented as "kept for SMS-side readers"). `list_job_applicants`'s handler, though, only ever read `app.coverNote` — meaning any application submitted through the website's own caregiver apply flow (which never sets `coverNote` at all, only `coverLetter`) silently came back as `coverNote: null` regardless of what the caregiver actually wrote.
  Fix: `list_job_applicants` now reads `app.coverLetter ?? app.coverNote ?? null` — canonical field first, legacy alias as fallback for any older doc that only has that. 2 new tests in `jobPosts.test.ts` (web-submitted application via `coverLetter`, legacy SMS-submitted via `coverNote`) — no test coverage existed for this tool at all before. same session: Evia was claiming zero caregiver availability when real, eligible caregivers actually existed — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (300/300 files, 3621/3629 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Direct continuation of the live matching investigation (#19/#20): after ruling out the crash and confirming the exclusion-list trim already exists (just deferred a call), Hamse traced the real remaining symptom by hand — a family's local area had exactly 3 real, eligible caregivers; 2 were legitimately excluded (already shown before, confirmed via the session doc's 'shownCaregiverIds`); the 3rd (Maria Santos) turned out to have `__seedTag: "cara-test"` on her record, meaning Evia was correctly filtering out test data that the website's Find Caregivers page has no equivalent filter for and shows anyway. So Evia's "no one available" was actually mechanically correct for that exact moment — not a bug — but the wording was still dishonest: it claimed zero availability when 2 real people existed, just already shown.
  Also confirmed via code trace (not build) that Evia's own "already shown" exclusion never blocks a *specific, named* request — 'schedule_interview`, `get_caregiver_info`, `get_caregiver_booking_rate` all take a caregiverId directly with no exclusion check at all; the exclusion list only affects the generic "find me matches" search tools. Decided (Hamse) not to remove the exclusion mechanism itself — repeating the identical pitch verbatim on every "show me more" reads as broken in a text conversation, unlike a scrollable website list — and instead fix the dishonest fallback copy.
  Fix, in `runMatchingForClient` (`matchingAgent.ts`): when the scored candidate list comes up empty, compute a separate `reofferable` list — real, eligible, non-hired, non-seeded, non-paused/opted-out caregivers who were excluded *specifically* because they were already shown/declined (not because they're ineligible). If non-empty, send an honest re-offer ("The closest matches near you are still X and Y — I'd already sent their info before. Want me to send their profiles again, or should I keep looking for someone new?") instead of the previous "I don't have anyone available" / "still actively searching" copy, for both the first-failure and escalated (`failureCount >= 2`) branches. Persists the reofferable list to `agent_sessions/{phone}.reofferableCaregivers` so the `find_replacement_caregivers` MCP tool (`mcp/server.ts`, the 'suppressConversationalSends` caller) can read it back and adjust its own `instruction` text the same way the existing `matched`/`pendingMatches` re-read pattern already works — kept the `MatchRunResult` return type (`"matched"|"no_match"|"failed"`) completely unchanged so none of the many other callers (bookingExecutor, replacementAgent, shiftOffer, triggerEngine, jobNotifications, commitmentTracker, permissionsConversation, routeIntent) needed touching. A truly empty area (no reofferable candidates at all) keeps the original honest copy unchanged.
  Tests: 3 new cases added to `matchingAgentRepeat.test.ts` — re-offers by name instead of claiming nobody's available, does so on both the first-failure and escalated branches, and confirms a genuinely empty area still gets the original honest "nobody available" copy with no `reofferableCaregivers` written.
  **Left as a decision for Hamse, not built**: Maria Santos's seed-tagged doc is real test data visible to real users on the live site (no code change made either way); this test phone number's own accumulated 'shownCaregiverIds`/`rejectedCaregiverIds` history was not cleared (a one-time Firestore data edit, not a code fix, if wanted for cleaner future testing).

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #20, same session: root-caused the ORIGINAL decline-the-applicant failure from earlier in this session — Evia used the wrong ID — FIXED (tool description), typecheck clean, related MCP suites green (61/61), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Direct continuation of follow-ups #15/#16/#20: after the confirmation-gate bug (fixed) and the grounding-classifier gap (fixed), Hamse retested the decline flow post-fix and got a genuinely NEW failure: `admin_alerts` → `cara_pending_action_failed` → `{"code":"NOT_FOUND","message":"Application not found"}`. Hamse pulled the actual `pending_actions` doc and shared its `toolInput`: `applicationId: "NdqUXibmLLUkvW4MRqvrkiFQbPN2"` — a 28-character value (Firebase Auth UID format), not the real application's actual 20-character Firestore auto-ID (`de7cJ4L7qvahNboD2c94`, found earlier this session).

This is a prompt-level mitigation (same class as the other tool-description fix earlier this session), not a runtime guarantee — the grounding-classifier fix from follow-up #15 remains the deeper backstop if Evia still gets this wrong and claims success anyway.

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #19, same session: Evia's caregiver-matching crashed for a real family even though the site showed 3 eligible caregivers in their area — FIXED (the crash), tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (300/300 files, 3618/3626 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse asked Evia "do you have available caregivers" and got "I don't have anyone available in your area right now" and later "I'm pulling up matches again now" — while the site's own Dashboard and Find Caregivers pages showed 3 real, eligible, bookable caregivers in the same area (Basra Yousuf, Imran, Maria Santos). Hamse found the real `admin_alerts` docs: two `no_match_found` (failureCount 1 then 2, same zip/city both times) followed by one `matching_run_failed` with `error: "(intermediate value)(intermediate value)(intermediate value).toLowerCase is not a function"`.
  Traced the crash to `computeRuleSignals` (`functions/src/agents/matchingAgent.ts`) — it assumed several caregiver/intake fields (`caregiver.city`, `caregiver.availability.hours`, `caregiver.gender`, `intake.city`, `intake.timeOfDay`, `intake.genderPreference`) are always plain strings before calling `.toLowerCase()`. `publicCaregiverProfiles` (the collection this function reads, migrated to earlier this session) passes several of these through verbatim from whatever shape the raw caregiver doc actually has — and the website's OWN scoring (`caregiverMatchScoring.ts`) already treats `availability` as `unknown` rather than assuming this file's `{days, hours}` shape, strongly suggesting real caregiver docs don't reliably match that assumption. A truthy non-string value on any one of these fields crashed the ENTIRE matching pass for every candidate, not just that one caregiver's score.
  Fix: added a small `asStr()` helper (`typeof v === "string" ? v : ""`) and applied it at every one of these call sites, so a malformed field degrades that one signal to neutral instead of throwing and failing the whole search. New test file `matchingRuleSignalsCrashSafety.test.ts` (5 tests) — confirmed each of these fields being non-string no longer throws, individually and all at once, and that a real score still comes out the other side.
  **Correction after further reading (same exchange)**: initially reported the exclusion list (`rejectedCaregiverIds`/'shownCaregiverIds`/`hire_decisions`-declined) as having no shrink/trim mechanism at all in this file — wrong. It exists (mirrors `find_nearby_caregivers`'s identical escalation), it's just deferred rather than inline: on the *second* consecutive failure (`failureCount >= 2`) it trims both lists to their last 3 entries and persists that to the session, but returns `"no_match"` for THAT call regardless — the trim only benefits a subsequent attempt, never the one that triggered it. Given the two `no_match_found` alerts Hamse found were failureCount 1 then 2 (the trim fired on the second), and the crash happened on the very next attempt after that, the crash fix above may fully explain the whole symptom on its own — the exclusion list had likely already been trimmed by the time it broke. Left as-is per Hamse ("okay") rather than also making the trim retry inline — a real but much smaller rough edge (a family has to ask twice before the system self-corrects) than what was first reported here, not built.

- **2026-09-07 (follow-up #18, same session: Evia's job-post finalizer never wrote the Care Plan "Notes" field directly, unlike the website's own wizard — FIXED, tested, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (299/299 files, 3612/3621 tests — 1 unrelated pre-existing flaky timeout confirmed passing in isolation), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse reported a note typed during Evia's SMS setup ("describe a typical day of care... what should a caregiver know?") never showed up under Care Plan > Notes for a recipient (Samira M) — confirmed live via Firestore console and a screenshot showing the job post's own description field held a generic template string, not what was actually typed.
  Traced both write paths side by side. The website's own wizard (`PostJobFlow.tsx`, mirrored server-side in `mirrorJobPostRecipientsToWeb`) writes the description **directly** into `carePlans/{uid}.recipientPlans.{key}.notes` on every submit — a first-class field. `buildAndSaveJobPost` (what runs when Evia finalizes a job post over SMS) never did this at all — it wrote `careNeeds`/`careLevel`/`conditions`/`age`/`relationship` into the same `recipientPlans` map but omitted `notes` entirely, leaving the Care Plan page's UI to fall back to reading `job_postings/{uid}.jobDescription` instead (a real but indirect fallback, not a first-class write like the site has).
  Also traced, at Hamse's prompt, exactly *when* this happens in the client onboarding sequence — confirmed the entire job-posting sub-flow (including the notes question) runs only **after** the client's membership payment succeeds: the Stripe webhook's `advanceOnboardingStep('payment', ...)` handler is what calls into the job-posting flow next, and its very first message is literally "Your membership is active! 🎉 Let's find the perfect caregiver..." before ever asking about the job. Two sub-paths converge on the same `buildAndSaveJobPost` call: a "pre-filled, just confirm" shortcut (skips the notes question entirely, using only what was collected earlier in intake) and the full step-by-step flow (ends with the notes question) — fixing the write in `buildAndSaveJobPost` covers both.
  Fix: added `notes: description` to each recipient's entry in the `recipientPlans` map inside `buildAndSaveJobPost` (`functions/src/agents/buildJobPost.ts`), reusing the already-extracted `description` local variable (`jobData.jobDescription`), matching the site's own unconditional-write behavior (including writing an empty string, not leaving the field undefined, when no description was collected) and sharing the same notes text across every recipient in a multi-recipient household (matching how careNeeds/careLevel/conditions are already shared at signup). New test file `functions/src/agents/__tests__/buildJobPost.test.ts` (3 tests: direct write, empty-description case, multi-recipient sharing) — no test file existed for this function before.
  **Not investigated further, flagged separately**: *why* Hamse's own specific typed note never made it into `onboardingData.jobDescription` in the first place (the job post's description field showed a generic fallback template, meaning the underlying value was empty at posting time, for reasons not yet traced through that specific conversation).

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #17, same session: new interview-feedback nudge, closing the "marked complete but no decision" gap — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (298/298 files, 3609/3618 tests — 1 unrelated pre-existing flaky timeout confirmed passing in isolation), NOT yet deployed).** Direct follow-up to auditing the interview-completion flow with Hamse: `complete_interview`'s own tool description says (verbatim) "MANDATORY: ...always ask how it went ... in the SAME reply ... never leave a completed interview without asking for that decision" — but that's a prompt-level instruction with zero runtime enforcement, and unlike "did the interview happen" (which already has `interviewCompletionNudge.ts` as a safety net), nothing ever followed up if Evia skipped that ask. The website has a passive equivalent by accident of its UI (the "Not Selected"/"Send Booking" buttons just sit on a completed-with-no-booking interview card indefinitely); Evia had no equivalent at all.
  Built `functions/src/scheduled/interviewFeedbackNudge.ts` ('sendInterviewFeedbackNudges`), a direct sibling of `interviewCompletionNudge.ts`: every 15 min, checks `video_interviews` where 'status === "completed"` and `feedbackSubmitted` isn't true, waits 1h past `completedAt` (matches the completion nudge's cadence), asks the family whether they'd like to move forward with the caregiver or pass, repeats at most once (3h later) if ignored, then stops — same session-check/opt-out guards, same 'sendViaInteractionAgent` pattern. Registered in `index.ts`. A "no" (`fitLevel:'no'`) decision naturally flips 'status` to `"declined"` (per 'submit_interview_feedback`'s own handler), so it's automatically excluded from this query without any special-casing.
  Tests: 'scheduled/__tests__/interviewFeedbackNudge.test.ts` (8 tests, mirrors `interviewCompletionNudge.test.ts`'s structure, including a new case specific to this nudge — doesn't fire once `feedbackSubmitted` is already true).
  Also earlier in this exchange, verified (not a fix, just confirmed correct): `cancel_interview`'s MCP handler has full parity with (and is more thorough than) the website's own cancel button — works for either party, records `cancelledBy`/`cancelReason`, texts the counterpart directly, and additionally handles a legacy "twin document" cleanup case (SMS-originated interviews stored in both `interviews` and `video_interviews`) the website never has to deal with. Noted for the record: `cancel_interview` is NOT wired into the runtime confirmation-gate system (unlike `respond_to_job_application`'s reject) — its "confirm before calling" is prompt-level only, so it was unaffected by follow-up #16's confirmation-gate bug either way.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #16, same session: THE root cause — every confirmed high-risk action in the product has been failing unconditionally — FIXED, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (297/297 files, 3602/3610 tests), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Direct continuation of follow-up #15: Hamse re-tested live and this time triggered the REAL confirmation gate (a two-word "yes decline" produced the genuine system-generated stub "I'm about to reject Basra Yousuf's application, and that can't be undone. Reply YES...", unlike the first, purely-conversational hallucinated exchange). Replying "yes" to that got back **"I tried, but it didn't go through. I've flagged it for review."** — a hardcoded string that only fires on a REAL, logged tool-execution failure. Hamse found the resulting `admin_alerts` doc, which carried the actual error: `PERMISSION_DENIED: "This confirmation is no longer valid. Please try the action again."`
  Root cause, found by tracing the exact sequence: `approvalHandler.ts`'s `executeConfirmedAction` calls `claimPendingAction()` first (an intentional single-fire transaction that flips the pending doc's status `awaiting → executing`, guarding against a duplicate "yes" double-dispatching), THEN dispatches to the tool with `_confirmedActionId` set. But the MCP gate (`mcp/server.ts`'s `handleToolCall`) re-validates that confirmation via `isConfirmedActionValid()` (`pendingActions.ts`), whose valid-status check only accepted `"awaiting"` or `"approved"` — **never `"executing"`**. Since the claim always runs before the gate check, the gate always saw `"executing"` and always rejected. This is not probabilistic or LLM-dependent — it is a deterministic, 100%-reproducing bug that has affected **every single confirmed high-risk/irreversible action in the whole product** since this mechanism was built: `cancel_job_post`, `remove_family_member`, `delete_account`, `cancel_subscription`, 'set_block_status` (block/report), `create_refund_request`, `delete_review`, `archive_senior_profile`, `review_shift_hours` approve, and `respond_to_job_application` reject (the one that surfaced it).
  **Why no test ever caught this**: the two halves of the flow were each tested in isolation against a false shared assumption. `approvalHandler.test.ts` mocks `handleToolCall` entirely (never touches the real gate). Every test anywhere that exercises the gate's `_confirmedActionId` bypass ('safety.test.ts`, `jobPosts.test.ts`, `family.test.ts`, `booking.test.ts`, `coverage-smoke.test.ts`) hand-seeds the pending doc at 'status: "awaiting"` and calls `handleToolCall` directly — never at `"executing"`, and never via the real `claimPendingAction` → dispatch sequence. `pendingActions.confirm.test.ts` tested `isConfirmedActionValid` in isolation and had in fact explicitly asserted the buggy behavior as *correct* ("rejects an executing action (already claimed for execution)") — nobody had traced that the real caller always presents exactly that state.
  **Fix**: `isConfirmedActionValid` now also accepts `"executing"` (`pendingActions.ts`) — `claimPendingAction`'s own transaction (which checks 'status !== "awaiting"` before claiming) is what actually prevents a duplicate dispatch; this check exists to catch a forged/expired/wrong-tool/wrong-phone/tampered-input confirmation id, not to re-police a claim that already legitimately succeeded. Updated the existing unit test to assert acceptance instead of rejection, with a comment explaining why. **Added the missing integration test** that would have caught this: a new describe block in `mcp/__tests__/jobPosts.test.ts` wires the REAL (unmocked) `pendingActions.ts` + `approvalHandler.ts` + `mcp/server.ts` gate together — proposes a real pending action, sends a real "yes" through `handlePendingApproval`, and asserts the job post actually gets cancelled and the family gets "Done." (not the failure ack). This is the first test anywhere that exercises the full real confirmation round-trip rather than one half with a hand-seeded shortcut.
  Also (from #15, same investigation): extended the `groundingClaims.ts` `action_authorization` classifier to include "declined"/"rejected"/"denied" (was missing from every pattern, so a false "Done, I declined..." claim — from the FIRST, purely-conversational exchange, before the real gate ever engaged — never even got classified as a claim worth verifying); tightened `respond_to_job_application`'s tool description in `mcp/server.ts` to explicitly require calling the tool for both accept and reject, and to never claim success without the tool having returned it.
  **This is the headline fix of the session** — a live, reproducible, product-wide bug where saying "yes" to literally any high-risk confirmation prompt from Evia always failed. Basra's application is still not declined (needs either a redeploy of this fix + a fresh SMS retry, or a manual decline via the website's Applicants panel in the meantime).

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #15, same session: live-test finding — Evia hallucinated declining a job applicant without ever calling the tool; closed the matching grounding-classifier gap — FIXED for the gap, root cause NOT fully fixed, typecheck clean, full functions suite green (297/297 files, 3600/3609 tests — 1 unrelated pre-existing flaky timeout confirmed passing in isolation), NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse asked Evia (over SMS) to decline a caregiver's job application; Evia replied "I can decline Basra Yousuf's application. Reply yes if you want me to do that," Hamse replied "yes," Evia replied "Done, I declined the applicant." The website's Applicants panel kept showing the applicant with full action buttons after a genuine fresh reopen (ruling out a stale one-time-fetch view), and Hamse confirmed directly in the Firestore console that `job_applications/{id}.status` was still `"pending"`.
  I have no live Firestore access in this sandbox (no Application Default Credentials configured — verified by attempting a real `firebase-admin` connection, which failed with "Could not load the default credentials"; `gcloud auth`/token commands are also blocked by the auto-mode classifier since they could expose live secrets) — so root-causing this required Hamse checking collections in the Firebase console on my instructions rather than me querying directly.
  Traced the intended flow: `respond_to_job_application` (decision:"reject") is gated by `pendingActions.ts`'s runtime confirmation mechanism — a real tool call should create a `pending_actions` doc on the FIRST turn (before any execution), and nothing in the codebase ever deletes those docs. Hamse confirmed the `pending_actions` collection had **zero documents** — meaning `respond_to_job_application` was never actually invoked, for either conversational turn. Evia fabricated completing an irreversible action it never attempted — a "hallucinated task completion," not merely a wrong claim about a fact.
  Found and fixed the concrete, precedented piece of this: `groundingClaims.ts`'s `action_authorization` category (already HIGH risk, meant to fail closed on an unverifiable claim — same infra hardened for schedule/availability claims in an earlier entry above) had every verb pattern EXCEPT "declined"/"rejected"/"denied" — so "Done, I declined the applicant" never even got classified as a claim worth checking, and sailed out ungated regardless of whether the underlying tool call succeeded. Added "declined"/"rejected"/"denied"/"turned down" to all 5 `action_authorization` regex patterns. 4 new test cases added (`groundingClaims.test.ts`, including the exact live-incident phrasing), 56/56 green in that file; `qaAgent.test.ts` + `humanHandoff.test.ts` (239 tests) re-verified green since this classifier runs on every candidate reply.
  **Not yet fixed — the deeper root cause**: why the model chose to narrate completing the action instead of ever calling the tool. The `action_authorization` fix above prevents a false claim like this from shipping *silently* going forward (an unsupported claim in this category should now fail closed to safe neutral copy instead of a confident lie) — but it doesn't address WHY Evia skips the real tool call in the first place. Flagged as a follow-up worth its own investigation (possibly: reinforcing in `qaAgent.ts`'s system prompt that describing an action as done must never happen without a real tool call producing that result this turn; possibly a tool-description wording issue in `respond_to_job_application`'s "Confirm before accepting" text, which only mentions the accept path).

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #14, same session: real live bug — site-initiated interview requests never texted the caregiver — FIXED, typecheck clean, full functions suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse live-tested the just-shipped site-side interview flow: sent an interview request from the client dashboard, caregiver got no text from Evia at all (in-app bell only, if that). Root-caused to `notifyCaregiverByText`/`notifyClientByText` (`notificationTriggers.ts`) — the shared "tell the other party by text" helper used by every booking/interview/application notification in this file, so the bug was systemic, not interview-specific.
  Two compounding issues: (1) these helpers passed `canDrop: true`, which still routes the send through 'sendViaInteractionAgent`'s 'shouldSend` "is now a good time?" judgment gate regardless of `urgency:'immediate'` — only the daily proactive cap is actually bypassed by immediate urgency, contrary to what an earlier same-day comment claimed; (2) far more direct cause of THIS failure: 'sendViaInteractionAgent` **resolves `false`** (not a rejection) whenever the recipient has no `agent_sessions` doc yet — exactly the case for a caregiver who has never texted Evia — so the `.catch(() => sendToPhone(...))` fallback never ran; the notification was silently dropped with no error, no retry, nothing. This is the same failure mode `checkr.ts`'s 'sendBgcheckNoticeToCaregiver` was written to avoid (its own comment: "sendViaInteractionAgent... would silently drop them... No session → sendToPhone").
  Fixed by replacing the two helpers' bodies with a shared 'sendTransactionalText(phone, message, sourceAgent)` that checks `agent_sessions/{phone}` existence up front — session present → 'sendViaInteractionAgent` with `canDrop: false` (matches every other must-always-deliver call site in the codebase: `checkr.ts`, `appointmentUpdated.ts`, `billing/approvalNoticeDispatcher.ts`), with the `.catch()` plain-text fallback now only needed for a genuine send failure; session absent → 'sendToPhone` directly, no reliance on a `.catch()` that would never fire. Every existing caller (new interview request, interview accept/decline/cancel in both directions, booking request/decline/cancel, job application, booking amendment, shift status) gets the fix for free since they all funnel through these two functions.
  Tests: `notificationTriggers.test.ts`'s `beforeEach` now seeds `agent_sessions` docs for both the caregiver and client phones (needed since the fix now branches on session existence — previously the mocked 'sendViaInteractionAgent` never checked this itself); added 3 new tests (`canDrop:false` on the interview-request send, and two no-session fallback cases — caregiver and client). All 26 tests in the file green; full functions suite 297/297 files, 3597/3605 tests (8 pre-existing skips) green.

Existing `mcp/__tests__/interviewOutcomes.test.ts` (13 tests) re-verified green after the 'server.ts` refactor.

- **2026-09-06 (interview-request optionals, live-test findings, and a systemic memory-vs-live-data grounding fix — CODE COMPLETE, full functions suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse pointed out the website's Request Interview modal has optional fields (related job post, notes, interview type) that Evia's SMS flow never offers. Live-tested a real interview end-to-end afterward and surfaced two more findings.
  1. **Optional interview fields.** 'schedule_interview`/`respond_to_job_application` (MCP tools) now accept optional `jobId`/`notes`, threaded through the shared `requestVideoInterview` (`functions/src/agents/videoInterviewRequest.ts`). `jobTitle` is now auto-derived server-side from the `job_posts/{jobId}` doc itself (overriding any caller-supplied value) rather than trusted from the caller — Evia's tool only ever has a `jobId` to work with. New/updated tests in `videoInterviewRequest.test.ts` and `mcp/__tests__/jobPosts.test.ts`.
  2. **Live-test finding, not a bug: caregiver got no SMS about a new interview.** Confirmed via Firestore that the test caregiver's `linkDelivery.caregiver.status` was `"missing_phone"` — both notification paths correctly no-op when `caregivers/{id}.phone` is absent. Test-data gap, not a regression.
  3. **Real bug found: Evia answered "when is my interview" from stale conversational memory, not live data**, even after being asked "are you sure" twice — Hamse had edited the interview's 'scheduledTime` directly in Firestore to test the completion flow, but Evia kept repeating the original date. Root-caused to the human-handoff grounding verifier's own design: a claim consistent with what Evia said earlier in RECENT CONVERSATION was rated SUPPORTED, which doesn't distinguish "consistent with what I said before" from "still true now" — breaks specifically when the underlying record changes via a channel outside the conversation (site-side edit, cancellation, admin override). Hamse then asked to generalize this ("shouldn't that be something for the whole site... checking specific category") rather than patch it narrowly for interviews. Fixed at the category level, using the existing `groundingClaims.ts` classifier (shared by every handler that runs a draft reply through the grounding gate, not just interview code):
     - Promoted 'schedule_appointment` and `caregiver_availability` from LOW to HIGH risk in `HIGH_RISK_CATEGORIES` (`groundingClaims.ts`) — an indeterminate verdict for these now fails closed to neutral copy instead of the old fail-open-and-send behavior.
     - Hardened `HANDOFF_GROUNDING_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`humanHandoff.ts`): claims about a scheduled item's current date/time/status, or a caregiver's current availability, are no longer ratable SUPPORTED on RECENT CONVERSATION consistency alone — they require TOOL RESULTS THIS TURN or the CURRENT MESSAGE, even if Evia said the same thing earlier this chat.
     - Updated `groundingClaims.test.ts` risk-tier expectations for both categories (was asserting "low").
     Known gap surfaced but NOT fixed this session: `interviewIdentityGuard`'s check runs on `reply` AFTER the human-handoff grounding check may have already swapped it to the generic handoff copy — so a wrong-caregiver mismatch could slip past the identity guard in that specific ordering. No live harm seen (the live test's caregiver targeting was correct), but it's a latent risk, not yet fixed.
  4. **New capability, not a parity fix: proactive "did the interview happen?" nudge.** Neither the website nor Evia previously followed up on an `accepted` interview whose 'scheduledTime` had passed (the site's only path is a manual "Mark as Completed" button). Per Hamse's answer (1 hour after scheduled time, repeat once or twice if ignored), added 'sendInterviewCompletionNudges` (`functions/src/scheduled/interviewCompletionNudge.ts`, every 15 min): asks the family (client-only, mirroring the site's client-only "Mark as Completed" control) whether the interview happened once 'scheduledTime + 1h` has passed and status is still `"accepted"`; repeats at most once (2 total sends, 3h apart), tracked via `completionNudgeCount`/`completionNudgedAt` on the interview doc. A "yes" reply routes through Evia's normal turn handling, which already has `complete_interview`. New test file 'scheduled/__tests__/interviewCompletionNudge.test.ts` (7 tests for the pure decision function). Registered in `index.ts`.
  Full functions suite green after all changes (typecheck clean, targeted + full `vitest run` green).
