- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #12, same session: interview-request rate limit now scoped to distinct caregivers, not raw request count — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, tests green, full suite verification in progress).** Hamse asked about the interview counter-proposal flow (caregiver proposes a different time via Evia, family agrees, Evia resubmits) and confirmed it's mechanically identical to the site's own request flow (same shared `requestVideoInterview`, same 5/day cap) — then explicitly said he doesn't want that cap limiting this negotiation. Root cause: `MAX_REQUESTS_PER_DAY = 5` (`videoInterviewRequest.ts`) was tracked as a raw request COUNT per client, with no regard for which caregiver — so every round of a legitimate back-and-forth with ONE caregiver burned the same daily quota as spamming five different strangers. Fixed by tracking distinct `caregiverIds` contacted today instead of a count: a caregiver already in today's set never counts against the cap again, so negotiation with someone you're already talking to is now unlimited, while the real anti-abuse concern (contacting many different caregivers per day) is still capped at 5. Rewrote the rate-limit tests in `videoInterviewRequest.test.ts` (the old ones exercised the SAME caregiver repeatedly, which is now deliberately uncapped) and added two new ones: unlimited same-caregiver repeats, and confirming the 5-distinct-caregiver cap still holds even when some of the 5 are repeats of an already-contacted caregiver. 12/12 tests green in that file.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #11, same session: recovery email now collected on /start itself, closing the web-vs-SMS parity gap — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, tests green, full suite verification in progress, NOT yet committed/deployed).** Corrected an earlier mistake in this same session: I'd told Hamse client email collection didn't exist anywhere, based on searching for a literal `client_ask_email` step name — wrong. It exists as a required field (`CLIENT_REQUIRED_FIELDS`, added 2026-09-02) collected dynamically by the qaAgent loop and written to `users/{uid}.email` at intake-confirm (`persistClientCareRecords`). The caregiver side has the equivalent (`CAREGIVER_REQUIRED_FIELDS`, written via `buildCaregiverProfileMirror`). The REAL gap: the **website's own `/start` signup wizard** (`OnboardingFlow.tsx`) never asks for it at all — only Evia's SMS conversation does — so a client who signs up via the website and never fully engages Evia by text ends up with no recovery email, exactly Hamse's own account's situation.

  Fixed by adding email collection to `/start` itself, threaded end to end: (1) `OnboardingFlow.tsx`'s name step gained an email field (both dark/caregiver and light/client tone variants), required, with the same whitespace-stripping validation fix applied to the job-posting wizard's recovery-email field earlier this session (mobile keyboard autocomplete can insert a stray space `trim()` alone won't catch); (2) `createWebOnboardingSession` (`index.ts`) now accepts, validates, and writes `email` onto `users/{uid}.email` immediately at account creation, and stores it on the `web_onboarding_sessions` bridge doc; (3) the bridge trigger in `webhooks.ts` (the one that seeds `name` from the web form into the new `agent_sessions` doc's `onboardingData` on first SMS contact) now also seeds `email` — so the qaAgent loop sees it as already collected and never asks again over text, avoiding a redundant question. Tests: 3 new cases in `handleInbound.routing.test.ts` (name+email seeding for both roles, email-only-no-name case), all passing (97/97 total in that file). No test for `createWebOnboardingSession` itself — `index.ts` has no test coverage anywhere in this codebase for its individual callables, same situation as other large orchestration files noted this session.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #10, same session: caregiver-doc phone self-heal — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, NOT yet committed/deployed).** Live-debugging Basra's "missing_phone" interview-delivery failure (confirmed a pre-existing test-account data gap, not caused by her `userType: admin` test setting — that's unrelated, confirmed by tracing that no caregiver-notification path checks `userType`) turned up a real, previously-undiscovered systemic gap: `caregivers/{uid}.phone` is written ONLY once, at initial doc creation (`onboardingConversation.ts`'s account-creation block) — the ongoing onboarding-data mirror (`buildCaregiverProfileMirror`, used for every ordinary field update afterward) never included `phone` in what it copies. So any account whose initial write was ever skipped/missed (old account, legacy path, edge case — exactly Basra's situation, confirmed live: her `agent_sessions` doc's `onboardingData.phone` had the correct number the whole time, `caregivers/{uid}.phone` never did) was stuck permanently missing it, with nothing ever able to repair it. Added `phone` to `buildCaregiverProfileMirror`'s copy set — any future onboarding-data update now self-heals a missing phone instead of leaving it silently wrong forever. No test added — `onboardingConversation.ts` has zero test coverage anywhere in this codebase (same situation as `webhooks.ts`, already noted this session); this is a one-line addition to an established, simple copy-pattern function.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #9, same session: end-to-end proof "ask for more → get more" actually works, caught + fixed one real bug along the way — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, new test green, full suite verification in progress).** Hamse pushed back on "are you sure" after follow-up #8's migration — reasonably, since that whole session had been reasoning from code-reading, never from an executed test (runMatchingForClient has no full-flow test coverage in this codebase). Built one: `matchingAgentRepeat.test.ts`, a real hoisted-Firestore-mock harness (mocks executionAgent, claudeMatching's scoreWithClaude forced to reject → exercises the real documented rule-score fallback, caraAgent, commitmentTracker, etc.) that actually CALLS `runMatchingForClient` twice in sequence against a shared fake session/pool of 8 bookable caregivers and asserts the second call's 3 picks never overlap the first's 3 (6 distinct total) — genuine executed proof, not a trace.

  **Caught a real, previously-undiscovered bug in the process**: when `runMatchingForClient` is called without its optional 'session` 4th argument (a real, supported pattern — the function's own fallback path re-fetches the session doc by phone when this happens), the code used that fetch's `rejectedCaregiverIds`/'shownCaregiverIds` but never captured its `userId` — so `clientIdForExclusions` fell through to `intake.userId` (frequently absent for these callers), silently skipping the `hire_decisions` lookup entirely and letting an already-HIRED caregiver resurface for exactly the calling pattern that fallback exists to support. Fixed: the fallback now also captures the fetched session's `userId` (`fetchedSessionUserId`), used as the second-priority source for `clientIdForExclusions`. Both tests green after the fix (2/2): the repeat-prevention test, and a hired-caregiver-never-resurfaces test using the session-omitted calling pattern specifically (which is what caught the bug).

  Full root+functions suite re-run kicked off to confirm nothing else regressed; not yet confirmed complete as of this entry. Nothing from today's session committed, pushed, or deployed.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #8, same session: matchingAgent.ts migrated to publicCaregiverProfiles + decline-permanence corrected — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, targeted tests green, full suite verification in progress, NOT yet committed/deployed).** Completed the "same requirements as the Dashboard widget" migration Hamse asked for, scoped down from the earlier bigger proposal: confirmed with Hamse this means the SAME DATA SOURCE/POOL/ELIGIBILITY (not necessarily the exact same ranking formula, since even the site's own two pages disagree on that already) — and confirmed asking Evia for "more" will genuinely extend past whatever a display cap shows (the dashboard's own hardcoded `limit:4` is just that widget's display cap, not a statement that only 4 exist; the underlying pool holds up to ~30 before slicing).

  **`matchingAgent.ts`**: switched its Firestore read from the raw `caregivers` collection to `publicCaregiverProfiles` (same pool the Dashboard widget, Browse Caregivers, and `find_nearby_caregivers` already read — Evia's SMS "find a caregiver" was the only surface still reading the raw collection). Fixed the field-mapping for the projection's shape (`backgroundCheckStatus` is flat on the projection, not nested `backgroundCheckData.status`; there's no 'status` field at all). Split into `hiredIds` (permanent — real decisions given the site the family had a standing relationship established) and `declinedIds` (folded into the existing soft/trimmable 'shownIds` list — a decline can be reconsidered later, same as a plain rejection already could). Fixed the exhaustion-escalation "trim to last 3" logic in both files to only ever protect `hiredIds` from trimming.

  Tests: `publicCaregiverProfile.test.ts` extended (zipCode/gender/canDrive passthrough), `findNearbyCaregivers.test.ts` rewritten (hired-permanent vs. declined-reversible-via-trim, 15 tests all green). Full root+functions suite run kicked off to verify nothing else broke; not yet confirmed complete as of this entry.

Implemented the same pattern as the pause fix: new trigger `functions/src/triggers/caregiverOptOutMirror.ts` (`mirrorCaregiverOptOut`, registered in `index.ts`) denormalizes `agent_sessions.optedOut` onto the caregiver's own doc (resolves via 'session.caregiverId` first, falls back to a `caregivers.where('phone','==',phone)` lookup) — this revives the previously-dead `caregivers.optedOut` field as a real, live-mirrored value. - **2026-09-06 (follow-up #5, same session: caregiver-search architecture audit → shared bookability gate now checks pause + website Pause Account UI shipped — CODE COMPLETE, root+functions typecheck clean, targeted tests green, NOT yet committed/deployed).** Deep-dived "how do we make Evia's caregiver search match the site" per Hamse's push for full transparency. Found the product actually has FOUR separate caregiver-scoring implementations (frontend `matchService.ts`+`aiMatchingService.ts` behind the real Browse Caregivers page, frontend `hooks/useNearbyCaregiversWithScores.ts` behind the Dashboard widget only — backend-ported as `caregiverMatchScoring.ts` for `find_nearby_caregivers` — and `matchingAgent.ts`'s own bespoke `computeRuleSignals`+family-history Claude re-score behind Evia's SMS "find a caregiver"/FIND_CAREGIVER intent). Recommended plan (approved): unify `matchingAgent.ts` onto `publicCaregiverProfiles` + the shared `caregiverMatchScoring.ts`, keep Evia's own family-history re-score layered on top — **that specific migration is still pending, not started** (deferred — see below). Extended the `publicCaregiverProfiles` projection (`publicCaregiverProfile.ts`) to carry `pausedUntil` through (previously dropped, would have silently defeated the new check for anything reading the projection). Confirmed real, was Evia-only (`pause_account`/`reactivate_account` MCP tools, SMS-only, no website UI) — Hamse asked for a website entry point doing the same thing. Built one: since a brand-new `https.onCall` would hit the confirmed-recurring GCP org-policy wall ([[project_functions_deploy_gotchas]] / [[project_password_gate_hardening]]), used the established Firestore-trigger-queue workaround (`account_action_requests` / 'services/accountActionQueue.ts` / `functions/src/triggers/accountActionQueue.ts`) instead of a new callable — added action type 'set_caregiver_pause_status`, dispatching to the SAME shared `pauseCaregiver`/`reactivateCaregiver` (`agents/pauseAccount.ts`) Evia's tools already use, so all three callers (SMS state machine, MCP tools, website) stay in sync. Tests added: `caregiverEligibility.test.ts` (pause behavior), `publicCaregiverProfile.test.ts` (pausedUntil passthrough), `accountActionQueue.test.ts` (new dispatch case). **Still open / not yet done**: (1) the declined-caregiver-permanence correction, (2) the real SMS-opt-out check (nothing checks `agent_sessions.optedOut` in matching), (3) the actual `matchingAgent.ts` → `publicCaregiverProfiles`/`caregiverMatchScoring.ts` migration itself (recommended, approved in principle, not started — this pause-gate work was a detour discovered along the way).

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #4, same session: caregiver-search repeat/already-decided bug fixed — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, 35 targeted tests green (14 find_nearby_caregivers + 21 pre-existing matching), NOT yet committed/deployed).** Hamse live-tested "is there more caregivers" and got the exact same 2 names back, and separately noted Basra (already interviewed, decision recorded in `hire_decisions`) could resurface too. Root cause: this product has TWO parallel caregiver-search implementations that never stayed in sync. `find_nearby_caregivers` (the newer qaAgent tool) already tracked 'shownCaregiverIds` on the session correctly. But "is there more caregivers" is classified as the `FIND_CAREGIVER` intent and routed by `routeIntent.ts` straight to the OLDER `matchingAgent.ts`'s `runMatchingForClient` (confirmed by the exact "Tap to view X's profile" message format in the screenshots, which only exists in that file) — and that function only ever excluded `rejectedCaregiverIds` (explicit "not interested" rejections), never tracked "already shown, no decision yet," and neither path excluded a caregiver with an existing `hire_decisions` entry (hired OR declined — either way, already decided) at all. Fixed both paths: `matchingAgent.ts` now builds a unified `excludeIds` = rejected ∪ shown ∪ decided (decided from a `hire_decisions` query by clientId), uses it in both eligibility filters, writes results back to the SAME 'shownCaregiverIds` field `find_nearby_caregivers` uses (unifying the two paths' memory), and mirrors that tool's existing "trim shown to last-3 and retry" exhaustion escalation (extended to also trim 'shownIds`, never `decidedIds` — a real decision never gets un-done by pool exhaustion). `find_nearby_caregivers` (`mcp/server.ts`) got the matching `hire_decisions` exclusion added for parity, both in its normal pass and its own trim-and-retry fallback. Added tests to `findNearbyCaregivers.test.ts` (hire_decisions exclusion, including under pool exhaustion). **Caveat stated to Hamse**: `runMatchingForClient`'s full orchestration has no full-flow test coverage in this codebase (only its small pure helpers, e.g. `isTemporarilyUnavailable`, are unit tested) — the new exclusion logic there mirrors the already-tested `find_nearby_caregivers` equivalent exactly, but wasn't given its own from-scratch test harness given the disproportionate mocking effort for that function's full side-effect graph.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #3, same session: family-satisfaction check-in reply now actually routed — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck clean, NO automated tests — matches this file's existing convention, see below — NOT yet committed/deployed).** Hamse asked what happens with the daily satisfaction check-ins ('sendFamilySatisfactionCheckins`, 'scheduled/familySatisfactionCheckin.ts`) after seeing one fire mid-conversation (unrelated coincidence of timing, not triggered by the interview work). Investigation found the job's own comment promises "a negative reply should be flagged for follow-up... on the client inbound path," but `awaitingSatisfactionReply` (the flag it sets) was written in exactly one place and read nowhere in the whole codebase — pure dead code, the promised routing never existed. Built it in `functions/src/linq/webhooks.ts`: new `classifySatisfactionSentiment` (quickComplete-based 3-way classifier, mirrors the existing `classifyFeedbackSentiment` pattern for post-visit feedback) + `handleSatisfactionCheckinReply` (logs to a new `family_satisfaction_replies` collection, raises a `createCaraOpsAlert` type `family_dissatisfaction_signal` severity high — queues an immediate admin email — only on negative, always clears the flag). Wired as a **side-effect-only** dispatch block right after the existing post-visit-feedback block, deliberately NOT short-circuiting the reply (unlike post-visit feedback, which fully owns its narrow single-purpose reply) — this check-in's open-ended phrasing ("anything we can do better") can easily carry a real question/request, so qaAgent still runs normally afterward and answers whatever was actually said. **Test-coverage caveat, stated plainly to Hamse**: confirmed via search that `webhooks.ts` has ZERO test files anywhere for ANY of its logic (including the sibling post-visit-feedback code this was modeled on) — this addition matches that existing (untested) convention rather than introducing a one-off test harness just for this piece.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up #2, same session: interview decline/completion notification parity — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck + targeted suites green (236 tests), NOT yet committed/deployed).** Hamse asked three concrete questions after live-testing the interview flow: (1) does the client get told when a caregiver accepts/declines, (2) does the time-passed nudge really trigger, (3) does marking complete trigger a follow-up about outcome/booking. Traced the actual code: accept always texts (via `interviewLinkTrigger.ts` for the confirmed-link message, plus an immediate confirm from `respond_to_interview_request` when accepted via Evia) — fine. Found three real gaps, all fixed on "fix all": (a) a caregiver declining via the **website** button left the client with only an in-app bell notification, no SMS — added `notifyClientByText` in `onVideoInterviewWrite`'s decline branch (`notificationTriggers.ts`), guarded by a new `respondedViaAgent` flag (stamped by `respond_to_interview_request` in `mcp/server.ts`) so an Evia-routed decline (which already sends its own text) isn't double-texted; (b) a client's "Not Selected" decision (website button or Evia's 'submit_interview_feedback` fitLevel:'no') left the **caregiver** with only a bell notification too, unlike the 'strong' path which already texts — added an unconditional `notifyCaregiverByText` in the same trigger's `declinedBy === 'client'` branch (no guard needed, since neither path independently texts on 'no'); (c) `complete_interview` was a silent status write with no enforced follow-up — the fit decision (proceed to booking / not selected) depended entirely on model judgment. Made it deterministic: `complete_interview`'s tool description (`qaAgent.ts`) now mandates asking the fit question in the same reply. Added tests in `notificationTriggers.test.ts` (website-declined texts client; Evia-declined via `respondedViaAgent` does not double-text; client 'not selected' texts caregiver not client) and `interviewOutcomes.test.ts` (`respondedViaAgent` stamped on decline). - **2026-09-06 (follow-up, same session: verified the interview-completion-nudge round trip and closed a real reliability gap in `complete_interview`'s prompt guidance — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck + targeted suites green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse asked directly whether a family can actually mark an interview complete purely by replying to Evia's nudge SMS — pushed to verify, not just assert by analogy to other nudges. Confirmed mechanically: `complete_interview` and `list_interviews` are both in `CLIENT_TOOLS` (neither is in `CAREGIVER_ONLY_TOOL_NAMES`), and `list_interviews` returns `interviewId`/`caregiverName`/'status`/'scheduledTime` — everything needed to resolve "the Basra interview" to a concrete id from a bare "yes" reply. But found a real gap: `complete_interview`'s prompt description (`qaAgent.ts`) never told Evia to look up the interview first when it doesn't already have the id in context — exactly the situation after this nudge, where the family's reply carries no id at all. Hardened the description to explicitly require calling `list_interviews` first whenever the id isn't already known this turn, and to never reuse an interviewId from earlier in the conversation without re-confirming it via `list_interviews` if the schedule/status could have changed since (ties directly into today's earlier grounding-category hardening for 'schedule_appointment`). **Honest caveat**: this is prompt guidance, not something a unit test can prove works every time — no existing test suite simulates the full "nudge sent → family replies yes → correct tool sequence" round trip as an LLM behavior (only the tool mechanics are unit-tested). Offered to build that simulation if higher confidence is wanted; not yet requested.

- **2026-09-06 (follow-up, same session: found and fixed the ACTUAL root cause of the stale-interview-date bug — CODE COMPLETE, typecheck + targeted suites green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse live-tested against production and reproduced a NEW variant: asking "can you check if i have interview" (a fresh conversation, real interview genuinely scheduled for that same day) got the generic human-handoff copy TWICE in a row — no answer at all, not even a wrong one. Traced it: `buildClientCoreContext` (the per-turn "cheat sheet" injected into every reply) has **no interview section whatsoever** — confirmed via a repo-wide search, zero references to `video_interviews` anywhere in `qaAgent.ts` outside tool handlers. So if Evia answers a direct interview question without calling `list_interviews` this turn, the draft has nothing backing it in CONTEXT, RECENT CONVERSATION, or TOOL RESULTS — the grounding gate correctly refuses to send an ungrounded claim and hands off instead. This is the exact same bug class as the CARE TEAM roster gap fixed 2026-08-31 ("who's my caregiver" going ungrounded), just never previously caught for interviews. Fixed by adding an INTERVIEWS section to `buildClientCoreContext` (`qaAgent.ts`): one new `video_interviews` query ('status in ['requested','accepted']`, i.e. not-yet-resolved only) alongside the existing care-team/care-plan queries, summarized as `INTERVIEWS: {caregiverName} on {scheduledTime} ({status})`, same minimal-formatting style as the existing roster line. New test suite `buildClientCoreContext — INTERVIEWS` (3 tests) in `qaAgent.test.ts`, mirroring the established CARE TEAM roster test pattern.
  **Second, subtler bug caught in my own earlier fix from today**, discovered while reasoning through why this section is safe to add given the 'schedule_appointment` grounding hardening from earlier today: that hardening's wording said a scheduling claim needs "TOOL RESULTS THIS TURN or the CURRENT MESSAGE" — accidentally **excluding CONTEXT** from the allowed grounding sources for this category. Since CONTEXT (`buildClientCoreContext`) is freshly re-fetched from Firestore at the start of every single turn — not carried over stale from earlier in the conversation the way RECENT CONVERSATION is — excluding it was wrong and would have silently undermined the very fix just made (the new INTERVIEWS section would never have been enough on its own to satisfy the grounding gate for a schedule_appointment claim). Corrected `HANDOFF_GROUNDING_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`humanHandoff.ts`) to explicitly re-include CONTEXT as valid support for this category, with the reasoning stated inline (CONTEXT is live/this-turn, RECENT CONVERSATION specifically is the stale one).
  Full typecheck clean (same one pre-existing unrelated `activityFeedMap.ts` error). Targeted suites green: `qaAgent.test.ts` (200 tests, +3 new), `groundingClaims.test.ts` (52), `coverage-smoke.test.ts` (49).

- **2026-09-03 (deployed, then rebuilt: the 6 new account-recovery callables hit a known GCP org-policy wall — COMMITTED, PUSHED, DEPLOYED, verified live).** Committed and deployed everything from the 2026-09-02 entry below. First deploy attempt: all existing functions updated fine, but the 6 brand-new callables (`v1-requestPhoneChange`, `v1-confirmPhoneChange`, `v1-startPhoneChangeVerification`, `v1-requestEmailChange`, `v1-confirmEmailChange`, `v1-deleteAccount`) deployed their code but failed "Unable to set the invoker for the IAM policy" — careconnex-d4c8b has a GCP org policy blocking public invoker IAM on brand-new Cloud Functions (documented in memory from the earlier password-gate work; confirmed recurring). Rebuilt as a Firestore-trigger queue instead, same pattern as `processAdminAdvanceQueue`: `accountRecovery.ts`/`accountDeletion.ts` are now plain functions with no Cloud Function exports; a new `functions/src/triggers/accountActionQueue.ts` (Firestore `document.create` trigger on `account_action_requests/{id}`, never needs public invoker IAM) dispatches by a `type` field and writes 'status`/`result`/`error` back; a new 'services/accountActionQueue.ts` client helper writes the request doc and listens via `onSnapshot` for the result, replacing the direct `functions.httpsCallable(...)` calls in `RequestAccountRecoveryPage`, `VerifyPhoneChangePage`, `VerifyEmailChangePage`, both Account Settings pages, and 'services/api.ts`'s `deleteUserAccount()`. New `firestore.rules` entry gates the two auth-required action types (`request_email_change`, `delete_account`) to the caller's own uid; the other four are reachable logged out by design. Evia's MCP tools were unaffected — they already called these functions directly in-process (inside Evia's already-deployed function), never hitting the IAM wall in the first place. Deployed and verified via `firebase functions:list`: the 6 broken callables are gone, `v1-processAccountActionQueue` (trigger) and `v1-expireAccountRecoveryRequests` (scheduled) are both live. Also ran `firebase deploy --only hosting` standalone to confirm the frontend deploy (the combined deploy's output didn't clearly show it). 292 functions test files / 3569 tests green (8 new for the trigger dispatcher). Both memory entries (`project_password_gate_hardening`, `project_phone_number_change_and_account_closure`) updated to record this as a confirmed-recurring pattern with a concrete second example to copy next time.

- **2026-09-02 (Account Settings page parity + full phone-number-change/recovery feature — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Audited `components/client/AccountSettings.tsx` against Evia; the FAQ block on Membership.tsx was also removed at Hamse's request in the same session. This grew into the largest single build of the parity effort — both a real account-security gap (no way back into a phone-only-auth account if the phone is lost) and several broken/fake account actions.
  **Site fixes:**
  1. **"Change Password" was fully fake everywhere it appeared** (`AccountSettings.tsx`, `ClientProfile.tsx`, `CaregiverAccountSettings.tsx`) — confirmed no account in this app has ever had a password credential (no `linkWithCredential` call exists anywhere), so even the two call sites that looked "real" (`authService.updateUserPassword`) could never have worked either. Removed the feature entirely from all three files, plus the now-orphaned `updateUserPassword` from 'services/api.ts`.
  2. **"Delete account" failed for every user, unconditionally** — reauthenticated via `EmailAuthProvider`, which no account has. Replaced with real phone-OTP reauth (new shared `hooks/usePhoneReauth.ts`, same `reauthenticateWithPhoneNumber` + reCAPTCHA pattern as `LoginPage.tsx`) in `AccountSettings.tsx` and `CaregiverAccountSettings.tsx`.
  3. **`deleteUserAccount()` only ever deleted the Auth login** — Firestore data and any active Stripe subscription survived, so billing kept running against an orphaned customer. New server-side callable `v1-deleteAccount` (`functions/src/accountDeletion.ts`) cancels the subscription (reusing `cancelSubscriptionForUser`), deletes `users`/`caregivers`/`customers`/'senior_profiles` docs and the phone's `agent_sessions` doc, then deletes the Auth user. 'services/api.ts`'s `deleteUserAccount()` is now a thin wrapper calling it.
  **Phone-number change/recovery (new feature, full build):** login is phone-OTP only, so losing the phone meant no way back into the account, and the existing "Edit" phone field wrote straight to Firestore without ever touching the real Auth credential. New `functions/src/accountRecovery.ts`: `requestPhoneChange` (email in → verification email out, anti-enumeration, Firestore-backed rate limiting), 'startPhoneChangeVerification` (token + new phone → OTP text, reusing `utils/phoneVerification.ts`), `confirmPhoneChange` (does the actual swap: `admin.auth().updateUser` phone, Firestore doc, clears the old number's `agent_sessions` doc, updates `family_group_members` references, sends confirmation SMS+email to old/new/email). New pages `RequestAccountRecoveryPage` ("Trouble signing in?" destination, one page for both roles), `VerifyPhoneChangePage`, wired into `App.tsx` at the previously-dead `/client/forgot-password` and `/caregiver/forgot-password` routes plus new `/verify-phone-change`. Added a "Trouble signing in?" link to `LoginPage.tsx` (there was previously no visible link to the recovery flow at all, despite the routes existing). Rewired both Account Settings pages' phone-edit to trigger this flow instead of a direct write.
  **Email-change verification (new, both roles, closes a real hole):** the email field is the recovery flow's entire trust anchor, so an unverified email edit would let anyone with brief account access silently redirect account recovery to an email they control. Closed the same way as phone: `requestEmailChangeForAccount`/`confirmEmailChange` in `accountRecovery.ts`, new `VerifyEmailChangePage`, and a rewritten `request_email_change` MCP tool — which turned out to be **an existing, separately-broken dead stub**: it wrote `pendingEmail`/`pendingEmailToken` to the user doc and claimed "Confirmation link sent," but nothing anywhere ever sent one (the code comment blamed "a separate triggered function," which didn't exist). Now calls the same real `requestEmailChangeForAccount`. Also gave the caregiver Account Settings page an actual edit affordance for email (was read-only before) using the same flow.
  **Required email at client onboarding (both paths):** caregivers already require email (`caregiver_ask_email`), clients never asked at all (checked web `OnboardingFlow.tsx` and Evia's SMS steps). Added `"email"` to `CLIENT_REQUIRED_FIELDS` (`onboardingContract.ts`) — required, blocks `complete_collection` the same way caregiver's does — plus a `FIELD_LABEL` entry (`onboardingDirective.ts`), a format-validation guard in 'save_onboarding_field` (client/email specifically, mirroring the zipCode safety-net pattern), and a write into `users/{uid}.email` inside `persistClientCareRecords` (`onboardingConversation.ts`).
  **Evia's own phone/email-change entry point:** rather than add a new tool (which would have pushed `CLIENT_TOOLS` over OpenAI's 128-tool hard cap), folded the capability into the existing `update_user_profile`/`update_caregiver_profile` tools as a `requestPhoneChange: true` flag — the old `phone` string param (a dead `pendingPhone` write, same broken-stub class as the email one above) is gone entirely. Freed one more slot by merging `cancel_subscription`+`reactivate_subscription` into 'set_subscription_status(action)`, same pattern as the existing 'set_block_status` merge.
  **Correction to my own audit, worth flagging honestly:** I initially reported two more "missing" Evia tools — identity verification and block/unblock — as gaps to fix. Both already exist and are fully built: identity verification is reachable via 'send_onboarding_link` (`linkType: "client_identity"`, already documented as usable any time, not just during signup), and block/unblock/report is 'set_block_status`, a comprehensive existing tool matching the website's `handleBlock`/`unblockUser` exactly, including the chat-hiding side effect. No work was needed there — good to confirm they exist, but I should have found this before reporting them as gaps.
  **Scheduled-function pass (goal 7 self-check):** the codebase has an established convention for sweeping short-lived records ('shiftOfferExpiry.ts`, `feedbackExpiry.ts`, `objectiveExpirySweeper.ts`) that the new `phone_change_requests`/`email_change_requests` collections were initially missing — added `expireAccountRecoveryRequests` (`functions/src/scheduled/accountRecoveryExpiry.ts`, hourly), deleting expired tokens via a new 'sweepExpiredAccountRecoveryRequests()` in `accountRecovery.ts` (no rollback side effects needed, unlike a shift offer — an unused link is just deleted). Also confirmed 'set_block_status`'s `report` action has a real site equivalent (`InboxView.tsx`'s "Report User" modal) — full parity, not over-built.
  New/updated tests across `onboardingContract.test.ts`, `onboardingDirective.test.ts`, `qaAgent.onboarding.eval.test.ts`, `handleInbound.routing.test.ts` (required-email fixture fallout), `pendingActions.test.ts`, `mutatingTools.test.ts`, 'subscriptionTools.test.ts` (merge fallout), `profile.test.ts` (rewired phone/email handlers), `accountRecoverySweep.test.ts` (new). Full functions suite green (291 files, 3559 tests), frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean.
  **Not yet done:** the confirm-email-ownership-before-first-trust open question from the original phone-recovery design doc is still open (v1 trusts whatever email is on file, which is now itself only verified going forward via this same change-flow); Evia's own SMS-side "ask for phone change" entry point still only checks `users`/`caregivers` `email` field casing loosely (tries as-typed then lowercased, no full case-insensitive index).

- **2026-08-31 (Membership page parity — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Traced `components/Membership.tsx` (subscribe, cancel, reactivate, FAQ) against Evia's `cancel_subscription`/`reactivate_subscription`/`get_signup_completeness` tools and the SMS membership-signup flow. Found and fixed: (1) **HIGH** — signing up for membership by texting Evia never linked `customers/{uid}.stripeCustomerId` (only a later webhook wrote the `users/{uid}` mirror), so a family who signed up over SMS and later clicked "Change plan" on the website would mint a second Stripe customer and a second parallel subscription — real double-billing risk. Fixed `createClientMembershipCheckout` (`onboardingConversation.ts`) to get-or-create the Stripe customer via `customers/{uid}` the same way the website's own `createCheckoutSession` does, whenever a `userId` is already known (confirmed it usually is by this point in onboarding). (2) `cancel_subscription`/`reactivate_subscription` were reimplementing the identical Stripe lookup+update inline instead of calling the site's own logic, AND wrote `users/{uid}.subscriptionStatus` — a field the real subscription lifecycle (`customer.subscription.updated` webhook) never touches at all (it writes `membershipStatus`), so the write was actively wrong, not just dead. Extracted `cancelSubscriptionForUser`/`reactivateSubscriptionForUser` as shared functions in 'stripe.ts` that both the website's callables and Evia's MCP tools now call — matching the site exactly: write nothing directly, the webhook is the single source of truth either way. (3) Found the same class of bug as tonight's earlier "cheat sheet" fixes, previously undiscovered: `buildClientCoreContext`'s ACCOUNT STATUS line read `u.subscriptionStatus` (the same dead/wrong field) instead of `u.membershipStatus` — meaning the ambient "subscription active" line has *never* correctly appeared for any genuinely subscribed client. Fixed. (4) `get_signup_completeness`'s membership check was missing the `'trialing'` branch present everywhere else (`checkClientAccessGate`, the website's own `hasActiveMembership()`) — a client mid-trial could get wrongly told membership wasn't active. Also verified (not bugs): the FAQ's payment-security claim (Stripe-hosted, no raw card data ever touches the backend — confirmed via the codebase's own PII-redaction patterns, which exist specifically to strip a card number if one ever appears in text) and the "What's included" feature list (all 8 items — messaging, AI matching, schedule tools, GPS shift verification, background checks, interviews, emergency support, cancel-anytime — confirmed backed by real, live, tested code, not aspirational copy). New tests: `mcp/__tests__/subscriptionTools.test.ts` (5 tests), `agents/__tests__/clientMembershipCustomerLink.test.ts` (3 tests), 2 new `buildClientCoreContext` tests in `qaAgent.test.ts`. Full suite green (290 files, 3554 tests), root contract test green.

Found and fixed 5 real gaps: (1) `review_shift_hours` had no way at all to resolve a caregiver's counter-proposal (`accept_counter`/`escalate`), with no auto-timeout either — a disputed shift over SMS-only could get stuck forever; rewrote the tool from `decision`/`correctedHours` to the real 4-action shape (`approve`/`propose_correction`/`accept_counter`/`escalate`) matching `reviewShiftHours` ('shiftHours.ts`) exactly, including independent start/end-time correction (not just a total-hours number) and `correctionHistory` writes so the audit trail matches the website's. Also found and fixed a THIRD, separate schema definition for this same tool in `mcpWriteActionAdapter.ts` (an action-ledger/idempotency layer) still on the old `decision` shape — updated it too, plus the `pendingActions.ts` high-stakes gate (added `accept_counter`, same payment-releasing effect as `approve`) and the qaAgent.ts prompt description. (3) `get_payment_update_link` checked `users/{uid}.stripeCustomerId` only — the codebase's own established pattern is `customers/{uid}` first (written the instant Checkout starts), falling back to `users` (written later on webhook) — could wrongly tell a client with an in-progress checkout "no billing account found"; also fixed its dead `return_url` to the real `/client/payments` route. (4) Separately, re-verified the routing heuristic fixed earlier tonight and found it still had gaps for casual texting shorthand without a "?" or question word ("Any pending timesheets", "Wrong time", "Not correct" all still read as trivial small talk) — added "any" as a question-starter and broadened the correction/pushback pattern to match "wrong"/"incorrect"/"not correct" anywhere in the message, not just as a sentence prefix. Test fixtures in `crudTools.test.ts` for `get_shifts` were quietly encoding the same fake field names as the bug — updated to real 'submittedStartTime`/etc. shapes so the test actually catches this class of regression going forward. Full suite green (288 files, 3544 tests), root contract test green.

- **2026-08-31 (cash-payment removal follow-up sweep: closed every leftover reference the original 2026-08-23 removal commit missed — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse spotted one stale UI sentence on the client Payments page describing a cash-payment flow that no longer exists; a full-codebase audit for leftover cash/Venmo/Zelle references then found the original removal commit (`ef5b3e3`) never touched 8 files. Fixed, ranked by severity:

Removed both description lines, plus the dangling `update_booking_payment_method` entry in `toolCapabilities.ts`'s `HIGH_STAKES_MUTATIONS`.
  2. **A whole live caregiver page still ran the removed feature.** `/caregiver/transactions` → `components/caregiver/CaregiverPayments.tsx` had a fully functional "How Clients Pay You" card — real Venmo/Zelle inputs and an "Accept cash" toggle, writing to `caregivers/{id}.paymentPreferences` and showing a "Payment preferences saved!" toast.   3. `components/HelpPage.tsx`'s caregiver FAQ still described "confirm receipt in the app" for cash/Venmo/Zelle — removed that sentence.
  4. `components/caregiver/CaregiverBookingsPage.tsx` could literally render the word "Cash" as a payment-method label (two separate spots) whenever `paymentMethod` was missing or a legacy value — simplified both to always show "Card" (matches the site-wide "credit only now" reality), removing the now-dead `isCard` branch and unused `Banknote` icon import.
  5. **`ClientDashboard.tsx` and `CaregiverHomeDashboard.tsx` had "Confirm Cash" / "Paid directly" sections for legacy, unmigrated 'shiftHours` docs.** The original removal's own migration (`migratePaymentMethods`, `functions/src/paymentMethods.ts`) backfilled `job_posts` and `appointments` to `paymentMethod:'credit'` but never touched 'shiftHours` — extended it to also backfill 'shiftHours` (admin-only callable; still needs to actually be **run** against production by an admin — extending the code doesn't execute it). Also simplified both dashboards' cash/card-split earnings logic down to a single credit-only total, so the UI is correct even before that migration is run, not just after.
  6. Dead-code cleanup: removed the unused `paymentPreferences` field from the `Caregiver` type (`types.ts`, the schema that only backed item 2's deleted feature), the unused `acceptsCreditCards` field (`types.ts` + its dead parse in `ClientCaregiverProfile.tsx` — confirmed never read), and narrowed two leftover `'cash' | 'credit'` type unions (`components/client/Payments.tsx`, `components/caregiver/CaregiverPaymentsPage.tsx`) to just `'credit'`, inlining the couple of remaining `paymentMethodLabel(...)`-vs-raw-check ternaries in `Payments.tsx`/`ClientDashboard.tsx` to a plain "Card" now that the helper is permanently degenerate.
