  **Not done, deliberately deferred (low-confidence/low-value per the audit):** `CaregiverOnboardingDashboard.tsx`'s harmless-today `isOffline`/`'cash'`-fallback branch (inert only because `paymentMethodLabel` is degenerate — flagged for whoever eventually deletes that degenerate helper, not urgent now); whether any consumer of `toolCapabilities.ts`'s `HIGH_STAKES_MUTATIONS` set asserts every entry maps to a real tool (not traced, likely a non-issue).
  Related: [[project_cash_payment_removal]] (superseded by this entry — the founder decision is now fully executed except for actually running the extended admin migration).

- **2026-08-31 (real production bug, found by Hamse live-testing: a fact-correction detector hijacking totally unrelated questions — PROMPT FIX ONLY, deployed indexes unaffected, CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse hit a run of plain questions — "Is my account approved", "Can you check if my membership is active", "Do I have a shift coming up", "Are you sure" — each answered with a completely unrelated, jarring reply: "I checked what I have remembered, and I can't identify that memory, so I haven't changed anything. Could you tell me what I said that needs fixing?" Traced to `functions/src/memory/learnedFacts.ts`'s `detectAndStageFactChange` — every single user turn (any role, gated only on identity being confirmed) is checked by an LLM call asking "does this message correct or ask to forget one of the family's stored facts?", and the (cheap-tier) model was sometimes answering `{"noMatch":true}` for messages that are plainly just questions, which returns the confusing canned reply and skips answering the real question entirely — the most disruptive failure mode of any bug found tonight, since it silently derails the whole turn rather than just giving a wrong answer.
  This one is architecturally different from tonight's other fixes: CLAUDE.md's own rule for this codebase is "intent/meaning must be judged by an LLM, never a regex/keyword list" — so unlike the medical-refusal bug, there's no hard, deterministic safety-net override available here without breaking that rule. Fixed the only way available under that constraint: substantially rewrote the detection prompt with explicit counter-examples (the exact phrases that misfired) and an explicit instruction to default to "not a correction" whenever genuinely unsure, since a false "can't find that memory" derailment is worse than silently missing an edge-case correction. **Honest caveat, unlike tonight's other fixes**: this can't be given the same regression-test guarantee — it depends on how the model actually responds to the improved prompt at runtime, not something a mocked unit test can prove. Existing 42-test suite in `learnedFacts.test.ts` still green (unaffected — none of it asserts on prompt wording). Full suite green (288 files, 3540 tests).

- **2026-08-31 (two more real production bugs from Hamse's live-testing: a misfiring medical-refusal block, and a caregiver-matching flow that never used real distance/eligibility — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse shared two more real conversations: (1) "Can you find me caregiver near me" got a hardcoded refusal — "Evia coordinates non-medical care and cannot search for providers..." — a message meant only for actual medical-provider requests (doctors/pharmacies), firing on Evia's own core job; (2) repeatedly asking for "any more caregivers" always re-suggested the same single caregiver (Imran), and two caregivers visible on the website's Browse Caregivers page never appeared through Evia at all.
  1. **Medical-refusal misfire**: traced to `functions/src/agents/intentClassifier.ts` — an LLM classifies each message into an intent bucket, and "find me a caregiver near me" has the same sentence shape as "find a cardiologist near me," so it sometimes got mislabeled `FIND_NEARBY_PROVIDER` (medical-only) instead of `FIND_CAREGIVER`. `functions/src/linq/routeIntent.ts` then hard-deflects on that label with a canned refusal, before Evia's real caregiver tools ever run. Fixed: tightened the classifier's own prompt with an explicit two-way disambiguation, and added a safety net — a new exported `isCaregiverSearchMisroutedAsProviderSearch()` in `intentClassifier.ts` that always overrides the medical-refusal path when the message literally says "caregiver," regardless of what the model guessed.
  2. **Matching used a fake distance and incomplete eligibility, unlike the site**: `functions/src/agents/matchingAgent.ts` (`runMatchingForClient`, used both by the direct caregiver-search intent and the `find_replacement_caregivers` tool) filtered candidates by exact city-string match or matching zip-code prefix — a crude proxy, self-documented in the old code as "no lat/lng in this flow" — and only checked 'status`, never the site's real bookability rule (`onboardingStatus`+`verificationStatus`). The website's own "Nearby Caregivers" logic (`caregiverMatchScoring.ts`, already byte-ported from the frontend, already used by a sibling Evia action) does real haversine distance + the canonical `isCaregiverBookable()` check. Fixed: `matchingAgent.ts` now geocodes the client's location once (`geocodeCityOrZip`, already existed) and uses real `haversineDistanceMiles` for both the candidate-pool radius filter and the rule-based distance score, plus post-filters every candidate with `isCaregiverBookable()` — falling back to the old proxy only when a coordinate is genuinely missing on one side. Deliberately deferred (Hamse's call): the separate bug where declined caregivers get re-suggested (a session/profile object mixup dropping `rejectedCaregiverIds`) and the missing "exclude this person by name" input — real, found, but not fixed tonight.
  New tests: `functions/src/agents/__tests__/matchingDistance.test.ts` (4 tests, real-distance calculation + fallback), `intentClassifier.test.ts` (+5 tests for the safety net). Full suite green (288 files, 3540 tests), root contract test green.

- **2026-08-31 (real production bug, found by Hamse live-testing: Evia honestly reporting a genuine backend failure, traced to missing Firestore composite indexes + one invalid query — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse shared a third live conversation: asked about upcoming shifts, Evia consistently (across 5 turns, including after "double checking") said "I can't access the live calendar right now." Unlike the two prior bugs tonight, this wasn't a hallucination — traced it to a real backend error: `get_upcoming_appointments`'s 'shifts` query (`clientId==, status in [...], date>=`, added in the 2026-08-30 booking-pipeline redesign) has no matching Firestore composite index, so it throws `FAILED_PRECONDITION` every time, and Evia was honestly reporting the failure. An Explore-agent audit of every multi-field query against 'shifts`/`booking_requests`/`appointments` across `functions/src` (46 tool calls) found this was one of ~20 affected call sites, several inside the scheduled reminder/nudge jobs already deployed earlier tonight (day-before reminders, 30-min reminders, morning briefings, weekly digest) — meaning those may be silently failing in production right now, since deploying functions and deploying Firestore indexes are separate steps and only functions went out. Also separately found: `upcomingVisitReminder.ts` passed `dateOp:"in"` with a 2-value array into `queryVisitsMerged`, which always adds its own 'status in [...]` filter — two `in` clauses in one query is outright invalid in real Firestore (not just a missing index), so that cron threw on every single run. Manually verified `chatRooms` (used by tonight's Messages/Inbox fixes) already has its required index — not part of this gap. Fixed: added 4 missing composite indexes to `firestore.indexes.json` ('shifts`: clientId+status+date, and a bare date+status; `appointments`: caregiverId+status+date ASC, and caregiverId+clientId+status — this last one also unblocks the caregiver-messaging relationship check in 'send_client_message`); changed `upcomingVisitReminder.ts` to a `>=`/`dateUpperBound` range instead of `dateOp:"in"` (today/tomorrow are consecutive dates, so the range covers the same window); added a permanent warning comment in `visitQuery.ts` against ever passing `dateOp:"in"` again. New regression test in `visitQuery.test.ts`. Full suite green (287 files, 3528 tests), root contract test green. **Important for the deploy step**: Firestore indexes deploy separately from functions (`firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes`, or a full `firebase deploy`) — the earlier `--only functions` deploys tonight would NOT have picked up an index change even if one had been made then. Newly-created indexes also take real time to build after deploying (not instant) before queries against them stop failing.

- **2026-08-31 (real production bug, found by Hamse live-testing: Evia confidently denied having data it actually had, in two separate ways — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed/pushed/deployed).** Hamse shared two real SMS conversations: (1) asked "What is the care address"/"the favorite activity" — Evia denied having the address twice and denied lifestyle data twice, then on a third push ("can you double check") revealed it actually had the full street address AND a lifestyle note the whole time; (2) asked "Who is my caregiver" — Evia answered with the FAMILY MEMBER's own name, then said it "can't verify," then on a third push correctly named the real caregiver. Root cause investigated and confirmed via an Explore agent trace, two independent bugs:
  1. **Routing bug**: `isTrivialQuickReply` (`functions/src/agents/qaAgent.ts`) decided whether a message goes to the tool-less "quick reply" fast path or the full tool-having agent based on a list of specific topic keywords — any short question that didn't happen to contain one of those words (e.g. "What is the care address", "I think you do", "Are you sure") fell through to the fast path, which structurally cannot see most of what's on file. Fixed: replaced the keyword-list approach with detecting the *shape* of a real question (has "?", or starts with an interrogative word) or a factual pushback ("I think...", "that's not...") — this always escalates to the full agent regardless of topic, closing the whole bug *class*, not just these specific phrasings. A carve-out (`GREETING_QUESTION`) keeps genuine rhetorical greetings ("How's it going?") on the cheap fast path. This fix is universal — it's one shared gate every message passes through, so it protects every page already audited this session (and future ones) without needing per-page changes.
  2. **Stale "cheat sheet" bug**: `buildClientCoreContext` pre-loads a short summary at the start of every conversation so Evia doesn't have to re-fetch everything on every turn. Two of its five sections were quietly incomplete: the CARE PLAN section was hardcoded to only `["careNeeds","notes"]`, silently omitting `locations` (the real address) and `lifestyle` (preferences) even though they live on the same doc — and the label claimed "(full, on file)" when it wasn't. Separately, the CARE TEAM roster section only ever queried the legacy `appointments` collection — a caregiver relationship living entirely in the newer `booking_requests`/'shifts` pipeline (which `get_care_team`, the real tool, already correctly checks) was invisible here, so the ambient summary went empty/wrong and Evia guessed instead of calling the tool — this is confirmed to be exactly what produced the "your caregiver is you" answer. Fixed both: CARE PLAN now includes address + lifestyle (lifestyle formatted generically off whatever keys are actually present, not a hardcoded field list, so a future preference type can't cause this same class of bug again); CARE TEAM roster now merges both pipelines, deduped by caregiver name. Unlike the routing fix, this one is **not universal** — it only covers these two sections because those are the only two sections that exist in this pre-loaded summary; Bookings/Messages/Care Requests/Calendar have no equivalent ambient summary at all, so this bug class structurally cannot occur there (their correctness instead comes from the routing fix + each page's own already-audited tool).
  New tests in `functions/src/agents/qaAgent.test.ts` (added ~15 new cases across `isTrivialQuickReply`, the CARE PLAN section, and a new CARE TEAM roster describe block, including a regression test reproducing the exact "Basra Yousuf" scenario). Full suite green (287 files, 3527 tests), root contract test green.

- **2026-08-31 (Messages/Inbox page parity: get_recent_messages was structurally broken and always returned empty, plus two smaller messaging gaps — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet committed).** Traced `components/InboxView.tsx` (shared by both client/caregiver Inbox routes) — real conversations live in `chatRooms/{sortedUidPair}` + `chatRooms/{roomId}/messages` ('services/chatService.ts`), never `threads/{id}` (that collection is only the Evia-assistant chat-widget mirror, `threads/cara_{uid}`). Found `get_recent_messages` (`mcp/server.ts`) queried `threads` and ordered by a field (`updatedAt`) that no `threads` doc has ever had — so it unconditionally returned zero results, meaning Evia could relay messages into the shared inbox (via 'send_caregiver_message`/'send_client_message`, already correctly wired to `chatRooms`) but could never read any of them back. Rewrote it to query `chatRooms` directly (exact deterministic-id lookup when `counterpartId` is given, `array-contains` scan otherwise), matching the site's `deletedAt`-based soft-delete filtering. Also added two smaller fixes: 'send_caregiver_message` now returns NOT_FOUND for a `profileVisibility:'hidden'` caregiver (mirrors `BrowseCaregivers.tsx` never surfacing one to browse/message — though confirmed no live UI actually lets a caregiver set this today, so currently a defensive/future-proofing fix, not a live bug); 'send_client_message` now checks the caregiver's own membership status (mirrors `useCaregiverGate`'s `gateMembership()`, which blocks a lapsed-membership caregiver from sending in the website's Inbox composer). One earlier-flagged "gap" was investigated and found to be correct parity as-is, not a bug: 'send_caregiver_message`'s lack of a client→caregiver relationship check actually matches the website's own real permissiveness (`firestore.rules` and `BrowseCaregivers.tsx` let a client cold-message any publicly-listed caregiver with no booking) — recommended NOT copying 'send_client_message`'s stricter IDOR-guard onto the other direction, since that would make Evia stricter than the site rather than matching it. New/updated tests in `mcp/__tests__/communication.test.ts` (28 tests total).

**Follow-on, same audit (2026-08-31): the website's OWN message box had no SMS fallback at all — a real bug, not an Evia-parity gap.** Asked directly "what happens when someone messages a client through the site" and traced it: a website-composed message writes to `chatRooms` and triggers a push notification (`pushNotifications.ts`'s 'sendPushNotification`, correctly bound) — but the trigger meant to text the recipient when they're not online, `onMessageSent` (`notifications.ts`), was bound to `threads/{threadId}/messages`, which only ever holds the Evia-assistant chat-widget mirror (`threads/cara_{uid}`) — so it fired on every Evia-chat turn and immediately no-opped, and never fired for a real caregiver<->client conversation at all. Net effect: messaging via Evia (guaranteed SMS delivery) was MORE reliable than the website's own message composer (silent unless the recipient had push notifications enabled and the site loaded). Fixed: re-pointed `onMessageSent` at `chatRooms/{chatRoomId}/messages`, reusing its existing recipient-lookup/in-app-notification/5-min-SMS-throttle logic unchanged. Added a `viaAgent:true` marker to `relayIntoSharedChatThread`'s message write (`utils/chatThread.ts`) so a message Evia already relayed by SMS doesn't ALSO get texted by this trigger (double-text guard, same pattern as the `agentTaskId`/`cancelledViaAgent` gates built earlier tonight). New test file `functions/src/__tests__/onMessageSent.test.ts` (6 tests) + 1 new `chatThread.test.ts` assertion.

**Last gap on this page: no Evia equivalent for the website's "Delete conversation" Inbox action.** Added `delete_conversation` (mirrors `chatService.ts`'s `deleteConversation` — sets `deletedAt.{userId}` on the `chatRooms` doc, self-scoped, resurfaces on the next message). Adding it as a new top-level tool pushed `CLIENT_TOOLS` to 129, one over OpenAI's 128-tool hard cap — fixed by merging `block_user`+`unblock_user` into one 'set_block_status` tool (`action: 'block'|'unblock'`), the same action-enum consolidation pattern as `manage_booking` (2026-08-30). Risk-gating preserved exactly: `block` stays confirm-required (moved from `ALWAYS_CONFIRM` to a `CONDITIONAL_CONFIRM` predicate keyed on `action`), `unblock` stays ungated, matching pre-merge behavior. Updated every reference (`pendingActions.ts`, `toolCapabilities.ts`, `qaAgent.ts` prompt, `launchActionParity.ts`, 'safety.test.ts`).

**One more, raised directly by Hamse:** pushed back on treating "mark messages as read" as too low-value to build — some people really will just text that. Added `mark_messages_read` (mirrors `chatService.ts`'s `markMessagesAsRead`: every unread message in the room gets `isRead:true` + this user added to `readBy`, then `unreadCount` resets to 0). This pushed `CLIENT_TOOLS` back over the cap a second time, so `report_user` was folded into the same 'set_block_status` tool as a third action (`'block'|'unblock'|'report'`) — same treatment as block/unblock: `report` stays confirm-required (was `ALWAYS_CONFIRM` pre-merge), matching exactly. Full suite green (287 files, 3511 tests), root contract test green. Nothing committed, pushed, or deployed yet.

- **2026-08-31 (CI regression from the booking-pipeline redesign fixed: `booking_amendments`/`hire_decisions` registered in the Evia-web contract — committed, pushed; no functions redeploy needed, registry/test file only).** Discovered via GitHub's failing CI check on recent commits: `tests/contractCollections.test.ts` (root-level, never run before this session's `firebase deploy's — only the `functions/` suite was checked) failed because Evia now writes `booking_amendments`/`hire_decisions` without a `CONTRACT_COLLECTIONS` entry. Both already had firestore.rules coverage and real frontend readers — just needed the registry entry. Also corrected `booking_requests.caraWrites`, stale since the same redesign made Evia write it directly. Root CI also surfaced one unrelated, pre-existing flaky test (`tests/mlSystem.test.ts`, a TensorFlow.js random-init boundary check, untouched by any session work, passes in isolation) — left as-is, out of scope. **Process note for next time: run the ROOT test suite (`npm test -- --run`) and `npm run build`, not just the `functions/` suite, before any deploy** — this session only checked `functions/` and missed a real registry gap as a result.

- **2026-08-30 (booking-pipeline redesign: Evia now writes real `booking_requests`/'shifts` instead of `appointments`, CODE COMPLETE across all 9 planned steps, full functions suite green, NOT yet deployed)** — Continuation of the same-day parity audit below: traced My Bookings (`ClientVisitsPage.tsx`) and the Calendar (`Schedule.tsx`), confirmed the website's entire "a booking exists" model is `booking_requests` (the negotiation/status doc) + 'shifts` (the actual visit records, joined via 'shifts.bookingRequestId`) — `appointments` has zero readers anywhere on the website. Wrote a full implementation plan in Plan Mode (`C:\Users\hamse\.claude\plans\misty-watching-whisper.md`), got explicit approval ("build it"), and implemented all 9 steps: (1) `hasConflict()` in `bookingExecutor.ts` now queries 'shifts` ('status in ['scheduled','in-progress']`) instead of `appointments`. (2) `executeBookings` writes one real `booking_requests` doc at 'status:'pending'` (matching `PostsPage.tsx`'s `handleSendBooking` shape) instead of `appointments`, stamping the new doc's id onto `agent_tasks.bookingRequestId`; added exported `writeConfirmedShifts()` helper (real, 'shiftBase`-shaped 'shifts` docs, joined via `bookingRequestId`) used by both the caregiver-confirm path and the no-phone fallback. (3)-(4) 'shiftOffer.ts`'s `onOfferAccepted`/`onOfferNotAccepted` `"booking"` branches now resolve/update the linked `booking_requests` doc (`accepted`/`declined`/`cancelled`) and call `writeConfirmedShifts` on accept, instead of writing `appointments`. (5) `handleRecurringConfirm` (`linq/inboundHelpers.ts`) rewritten to update the SAME `booking_requests` doc's 'schedule.ongoing`/`dayShiftTimes` (the website's own real recurring mechanism — it has no separate recurring-schedule collection) instead of creating a parallel `recurring_schedules` doc + a batch of `appointments`; the site's own `generateRollingShifts` daily job takes over from there. (6) Swap acceptance in `onOfferAccepted`'s `"swap"` branch now does the same dual `appointments`-or-'shifts` lookup 'start_shift`/`complete_shift` already used, so a swap resolves correctly regardless of which collection the visit lives in. (7)-(8) New tools for time-changes and cancellations, matching My Bookings/Calendar exactly: `request_schedule_amendment` (client asks for a one-off time change or a new recurring day — writes a `booking_amendments` doc identical to the Calendar's own "+Request Visit" modal) and `respond_to_schedule_amendment` (caregiver accepts/declines — accepting replicates `CaregiverBookingsPage.tsx`'s `handleAcceptAmendment` shift-generation loop verbatim, using newly-exported `nextOccurrenceOnOrAfter`/`addDays` from 'shiftGenerator.ts`); a single consolidated `manage_booking` tool (action enum: `cancel_pending_request` / `cancel_whole_booking` / `cancel_visit` / `cancel_pending_amendment` / `resend_booking`) covers every cancel/resend action — consolidated into one tool schema (rather than 5 separate ones) specifically to stay under OpenAI's 128-tool-per-role hard cap enforced by `parity.test.ts` (adding all 7 new tools as separate schemas pushed `CLIENT_TOOLS` to 132; consolidating brought it to 126). `respond_to_schedule_amendment` is caregiver-only (added to `CAREGIVER_TOOL_NAMES`/`CAREGIVER_ONLY_TOOL_NAMES`). (9) Deliberately did NOT duplicate booking-creation logic inside 'submit_interview_feedback`'s `"strong"` branch — the website itself treats "hire decision" and "send booking" as two separate steps (a separate booking-details modal collecting rate/schedule/address sits between them), and `request_booking` (fixed by steps 1-2 above) already goes through the exact tested `pending → caregiver-confirms → shifts` pipeline steps 2-3 built. Instead extended the 'submit_interview_feedback` tool-documentation line in `qaAgent.ts` to tell Evia a "strong" result is the hire decision only, and to walk the family through `get_caregiver_booking_rate`/`quote_booking`/`request_booking` next if they want to book immediately — avoiding a second, divergent booking-write path. New `AuditEventType's added (`booking_request_cancelled`, 'shift_cancelled`, `booking_resent`, `amendment_cancelled`, `amendment_requested`, `amendment_declined`, `amendment_accepted`, `interview_completed`) plus matching `activityFeedMap.ts` policy entries (family-initiated actions included; caregiver-keyed accept/decline excluded, same reasoning as `booking_request_responded`). New test file `functions/src/mcp/__tests__/manageBooking.test.ts` (16 tests: every `manage_booking` action, `request_schedule_amendment`'s write shape, `respond_to_schedule_amendment`'s accept/decline/ownership/re-decide guards). Full functions suite green throughout (`npm run typecheck`, `npm run typecheck:all`, `npx vitest run`: 281 files, 3424 passed, 8 skipped — one `client.protocol.test.ts` failure seen in one full-suite run was confirmed flaky/order-dependent by re-running both that file alone and the full suite again, both fully green, unrelated to any change in this entry). Named, deliberate follow-on gaps (not built, not blocking): the ~13 scheduled reminder jobs and `get_upcoming_appointments` still only read `appointments` (won't notice an Evia-originated booking until given a parallel 'shifts` check); `reschedule_appointment`/'shiftTimeChange.ts` and the caregiver-callout replacement flow are untouched, still `appointments`-only. **OWED**: commit + push + deploy this entry together with everything below (all local/uncommitted per explicit instruction — "we will deploy together after"); live end-to-end verification once deployed (book via SMS → confirm as caregiver → check website Browse Caregivers badge/My Bookings/Calendar; cancel whole booking and one visit from the website; confirm recurring "yes" keeps the site's own generator producing shifts; request+accept a time change via text and confirm the linked shift appears correctly).

- **2026-08-31 (My Care Team page parity: get_care_team rewritten to match the site's real active/past logic and both booking pipelines — committed, pushed, and deployed (commit 59072f0, 2026-08-31))** — Traced `components/client/MyCareTeam.tsx` — the page itself is read-only (Message/Profile/Re-book are all just navigation to other already-covered pages), so the only thing to verify was whether Evia's "who's on my care team" equivalent (`get_care_team`) matched. It didn't: confirmed `get_care_team` was still 100% `appointments`-only — the exact same class of bug already fixed for the ~20 scheduled jobs earlier this session — so a caregiver booked through the newer `booking_requests`/'shifts` pipeline was invisible to it entirely. The site's own logic is also more sophisticated than Evia's old version: a caregiver only counts as "active" if their `booking_requests` doc is `accepted` AND still has a 'scheduled` shift (an accepted booking can run out of visits and become effectively past), one card per caregiver ever (active always wins over past for the same caregiver), and it shows who each caregiver is caring for. Rewrote `get_care_team` to replicate this exactly — merging `booking_requests`+'shifts` (new pipeline) with the legacy `appointments` collection (for bookings that predate today's migration), same active/past dedupe rule, added `active`/`caringFor` fields to the response. New test file `mcp/__tests__/getCareTeam.test.ts` (6 tests: new-pipeline active, accepted-but-shifts-ran-out counts as past, cancelled counts as past, no double-listing when both an old appointment and a new booking exist for the same caregiver, legacy-only caregiver still shows up, empty case). Full suite green (`npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run`: 286 files, 3483 passed, 8 skipped).

- **2026-08-31 (Care Plan page parity: emergency-contacts collection bug, lifestyle/location editing, add/remove care recipient — committed, pushed, and deployed (commit 59072f0, 2026-08-31))** — Traced `components/CarePlan.tsx` (the real Care Plan page, `/client/care-plan`) for the first time this session and found three real, pre-existing gaps between it and Evia's `get_care_plan`/`update_care_plan`/`create_senior_profile` tools — verified directly in both the frontend component and the MCP tool code before touching anything, not just from the page-trace report:
  1. **Emergency contacts collection mismatch (a live sync bug, not a missing feature).** The Care Plan page's Emergency Contacts card is bound to `plan` state sourced ONLY from the OLDER `care_plans` (snake_case) doc via `dbService.subscribeToCarePlan`/`updateCarePlan` ('services/api.ts`) — confirmed by reading `CarePlan.tsx:244-254` directly (the newer `carePlans`, camelCase, `onSnapshot` only ever populates `recipientPlans`/`locationPool`/`carePlanReviewedAt`, never `plan.emergencyContacts`). But `get_care_plan`/`update_care_plan` read/wrote ONLY the camelCase `carePlans` doc — its own comment even asserted the snake_case collection was "completely disconnected, nothing on the site ever reads or writes" it, which is true for every OTHER field but not this one. Result: an emergency contact added via Evia was invisible on the website's own Care Plan page, and vice versa. Fixed: `update_care_plan`'s `emergencyContacts` field now targets `care_plans` (snake_case, matching the site's real write); `get_care_plan` fetches both docs and overlays the snake_case doc's `emergencyContacts` onto the camelCase result so Evia sees exactly what the family sees.
  2. **"Lifestyle & Preferences" and "Care Location" had no Evia write path at all.** `update_care_plan`'s `ALLOWED_FIELDS` was `["careNeeds","notes","emergencyContacts","accessCodes"]` — confirmed directly, no lifestyle/location. Added both: `lifestyle` accepts a partial object (any subset of the site's `LifestyleData` fields) and shallow-merges it into the existing `recipientPlans.{key}.lifestyle` (read-then-write, so a partial update never wipes sibling lifestyle fields) via the SAME dotted-path write convention `careNeeds`/`notes` already use (deliberately NOT a nested-object-literal write, to avoid relying on assumptions about Firestore's recursive merge depth); `careLocation` accepts `{street, city, state, zipCode}`, geocodes via the existing `lookupZipPlace` (zip-centroid precision, same as `create_job_post`'s address handling), and writes both the recipient's `locations` array and the shared `locationPool` (deduped by street+zip), matching the site's own shape.
  3. **Adding/removing a care recipient via Evia didn't actually work.** `create_senior_profile`'s own description already claimed to do the same thing as the Care Plan page's "+ Add" button, but it only ever wrote to 'senior_profiles` — a collection genuinely used elsewhere in Evia's own model (family group, care team, permissions — confirmed extensively used, NOT abandoned) but never read by the Care Plan/Booking pages' actual recipient roster, which lives entirely in `job_postings.careRecipientFirstName`/`.additionalRecipients[]` (confirmed via `CarePlan.tsx`'s own `recipients` memo). So an Evia-added recipient never appeared as a tab anywhere on the site. Fixed by keeping the existing 'senior_profiles` write (unchanged, for whatever else depends on it) and ADDING a mirror write into `job_postings` (primary fields if it's the household's first recipient, else `additionalRecipients` array) plus seeding a blank `carePlans.recipientPlans.{key}` entry, matching `CarePlan.tsx`'s own 'saveNewRecipient()` exactly. Removal had no Evia tool at all — added `remove_care_recipient`, matching `deleteRecipient()`'s real logic (promotes the next additional recipient to primary if removing the primary, or clears the primary fields entirely if none remain; archives to `deletedRecipients`; refuses to remove a household's only recipient; leaves `recipientPlans.{key}` orphaned rather than deleted, matching the site's own behavior, not an Evia shortcut).
  New test file `mcp/__tests__/carePlanParity.test.ts` (14 tests) covering all three fixes, including the dotted-path sibling-field-preservation checks and the primary-recipient-promotion logic. `remove_care_recipient` registered in both `toolCapabilities.ts` registries and documented in `qaAgent.ts`'s prompt (avoiding the same hard-guard test failures seen earlier this session). Full suite green (`npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run`: 285 files, 3477 passed, 8 skipped).

- **2026-08-30 (notification-trigger SMS parity: the website's own booking_requests/shifts/booking_amendments/video_interviews/job_applications triggers now text, not just in-app-notify — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet deployed)** — Prompted by a user question about whether a website-side cancellation notifies the other party the same way an Evia-side one does. Investigation found the real gap: `functions/src/triggers/notificationTriggers.ts` (`onBookingRequestWrite`, `onBookingAmendmentWrite`, `onShiftStatusChanged`, `onVideoInterviewWrite`, `onJobApplicationCreate`) only ever wrote an in-app notification (`users/{uid}/notifications`) — no text, ever, for ANY of these events, regardless of whether the website or Evia made the write. This is a pre-existing site limitation, not something Evia introduced — contrasted with the legacy `appointments` collection's own trigger (`onAppointmentUpdated`), which already texts for the equivalent events (confirmed, arrival, completed, caregiver-cancelled). Fixed by adding two helpers (`notifyCaregiverByText`/`notifyClientByText` — 'sendViaInteractionAgent` with a 'sendToPhone` fallback, matching `onAppointmentUpdated`'s own send pattern) and wiring them into every status-change branch across all 5 triggers, **except** where an existing, richer Evia-side message already covers the same event (verified case by case, not assumed): `respond_to_interview_request` (accept/decline, has callUrl/proposed-time), `cancel_interview`, and 'shiftOffer.ts`'s `onOfferAccepted`/`onOfferNotAccepted` for Evia-negotiated bookings — the last one handled by gating the trigger's new-request/declined booking-requests texts on `!after.agentTaskId` (only fires for a pure website-created booking with no Evia negotiation counterpart; an Evia-negotiated one already gets a richer YES/NO or outcome text from `createShiftOffer`/'shiftOffer.ts`). Two pre-existing manual sends duplicated a newly-added trigger text and were removed in favor of the trigger as sole source of truth: `apply_to_job`'s client SMS (now `onJobApplicationCreate`'s) and `request_schedule_amendment`'s caregiver SMS (now `onBookingAmendmentWrite`'s new-amendment branch's). New texts added with no prior duplicate: `onVideoInterviewWrite`'s new-request-to-caregiver; `onBookingAmendmentWrite`'s accepted/declined-to-client; `onShiftStatusChanged`'s in-progress ("arrived") and completed-to-client (parity with `onAppointmentUpdated`'s arrival/completion texts — 'start_shift`/`complete_shift` never messaged the family themselves) and extra-visit accepted/declined-to-client. Deliberately NOT added: `onShiftStatusChanged`'s caregiver-cancelled-notifies-client branch, since `onShiftUpdated` (this session's earlier fix) already sends a richer replacement-search message for that exact event — adding a second would double-text. New test file `triggers/__tests__/notificationTriggers.test.ts` (16 tests) covering every new text and, just as importantly, every case that must NOT fire (agentTaskId-gated, bulk-cancelled, caregiver-cancelled). Full suite green (`npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run`: 284 files, 3460 passed, 8 skipped).

- **2026-08-30 (the last open item: all ~20 scheduled reminder/proactive jobs now recognize the shifts pipeline too — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet deployed)** — Closed the one remaining gap named in every status check this session: the scheduled jobs that send reminder texts, briefings, and proactive nudges were all still reading only `appointments`, so a family/caregiver whose booking lives in the newer 'shifts` pipeline got a fully working booking/cancel/reschedule/callout experience but silently got NONE of Evia's own reminder texts before their visit (the website's own reminders were unaffected — this was purely an Evia-SMS-side gap). Built a shared helper, `functions/src/utils/visitQuery.ts` (`queryVisitsMerged` — runs the appointments+shifts queries in parallel and merges into one real `QueryDocumentSnapshot[]` so `.id`/`.data()`/`.ref` all behave identically to a single-collection query, `dateOp`/`dateValue`/`dateUpperBound`/`extraWhere`/`limit` cover every query shape found across the 19 files; `getVisitDoc` for by-id dual-lookup; `visitSeniorName` for the appointments-'seniorName`-vs-shifts-`careRecipients[0].name` field mismatch), fully unit-tested (9 tests) before touching any call site, then applied it to every file: `caregiverInactivityCheck.ts`, `clientDayBeforeReminder.ts`, `dayBeforeShiftReminder.ts`, `firstVisitActivation.ts`, `healthTrends.ts`, `proactiveReflection.ts`, `inShiftUpdate.ts`, `nextDayFamilyFeedback.ts`, `morningBriefing.ts` (both its queries), `clientThirtyMinReminder.ts`, `thirtyMinShiftReminder.ts`, 'shiftTaskNudges.ts`, `nightlyMemory.ts`'s `analyzeBookingPatterns`, `preShiftFamilyCheckin.ts`, `noVisitCheck.ts`, `upcomingVisitReminder.ts`, `weeklyDigest.ts`, `wowMomentsJob.ts`, and `triggerEngine.ts`'s no-show/arrival-capture sweep. Two files (`healthTrends.ts`, `proactiveReflection.ts`, `weeklyDigest.ts`) needed a bespoke fix instead of the shared helper because they query `appointments.isoDate` (a full-ISO-timestamp field shifts doesn't have) rather than the plain `date` field every other file uses — queried shifts separately on `date` at day granularity and merged/sorted by hand. `firstVisitActivation.ts` and `wowMomentsJob.ts` needed a direct two-collection existence check instead of the helper (no status filter at all — "has this family ever booked anything"). Found and fixed one more real, connected gap while doing this: `dayBeforeShiftReminder.ts`/`thirtyMinShiftReminder.ts` stash a `pendingShiftConfirmation.appointmentId` on the caregiver's session for their day-before/30-min "still coming?" reply; the reply handler, `routeCaregiver.ts`'s `handleShiftConfirmation`, was itself hardcoded to `appointments` for both the CONFIRM/CANCEL writes and the no-show replacement read — fixed via `getVisitDoc`. `recurringScheduler.ts` (the old recurring-booking generator, writes NEW `appointments` docs) was deliberately left untouched — new recurring bookings go through the website's own `generateRollingShifts` now (this session's earlier `handleRecurringConfirm` work); extending this old writer to also emit 'shifts` would race the site's own generator. `triggerEngine.ts`'s `runEmergencyReplacement`/`replacementAgent.ts` needed no changes at all — confirmed it never touches the original visit doc directly (only carries `appointmentId` as an opaque reference through `agent_tasks`), and the eventual "family picked a replacement" confirmation already routes through `createBookingTask`/`executeBookings` (fixed earlier this session). Full functions suite green throughout every step (`npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run`: 283 files, 3444 passed, 8 skipped — no existing test needed updating; either the touched files had no dedicated test yet, or their mocks were already collection-name-agnostic). This closes every item on the standing "is the goal done for these pages" checklist raised across today's session — every action AND every proactive/scheduled job tied to a booking now works identically regardless of which pipeline created it.

- **2026-08-30 (follow-on shifts-parity sweep: get_upcoming_appointments, reschedule, and caregiver-cancellation/replacement now recognize the newer shifts pipeline — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet deployed)** — Re-checking "does every scheduled/triggered thing tied to a booking still work" after today's booking-pipeline redesign surfaced 3 more real gaps, investigated and fixed in order of how much they touch:
  1. **`get_upcoming_appointments`** (mcp/server.ts) only ever queried `appointments` — a family asking Evia "what visits do I have coming up" got told nothing was scheduled if their booking was made via the new pipeline. Fixed: queries `appointments` and 'shifts` in parallel, merges, sorts by date, same 5-result/hasMore shape.
  2. **`reschedule_appointment`** (mcp/server.ts) + its executor 'shiftTimeChange.ts`'s `requestShiftTimeChange`, plus 'shiftOffer.ts`'s `onOfferAccepted`/`onOfferNotAccepted` `"time_change"` branches — all appointments-only (lookup, conflict-check, and the actual date/time write on accept). Added the same dual-lookup pattern already used for `"swap"` offers. Confirmed via direct site-code read: the website itself has **no reschedule button at all** for an existing visit — its only mechanism is cancel-then-request-a-new-visit (already matched by `request_schedule_amendment`). `reschedule_appointment` is a pure Evia-only shortcut with no website equivalent, kept because it's a nicer one-sentence flow over SMS.
  3. **Caregiver-callout / emergency-replacement — a bigger, corrected investigation.** Initial framing (3 MCP tools — `get_callout_backups`/'select_callout_backup`/`request_callout_refund` — plus the website's `useCaregiverCallout.ts` hook) turned out to be **wrong**: tracing the actual data flow found the only code that ever wrote what those tools/that hook read (`backupCaregiverOptions`, a `type:'callout'` notification) lives inside `caregiverCallout.ts`'s `onCaregiverCallout` trigger, which is **deliberately dead** (`return null` at the top, comment: "kept as an inert compatibility shim"). That whole tools+hook system is unused today regardless of collection — not worth extending. The REAL, live system is completely different: `triggers/appointmentUpdated.ts`'s `onAppointmentUpdated` trigger detects a caregiver cancellation and calls `handleCaregiverCancellation`, which texts the family directly with numbered replacement options (or REPLACE/SKIP for >24h-out cancellations) — no website popup involved. That trigger only watches `appointments`, so a caregiver cancelling a 'shifts`-based visit triggered **nothing at all** — no family alert, no backup search. Separately, `agents/caregiverCancelShiftHandler.ts` (the caregiver's own "I need to cancel my shift" SMS flow) also only ever listed/cancelled `appointments` docs — a caregiver literally could not cancel a shifts-based visit by texting Evia. Fixed both: `caregiverCancelShiftHandler.ts` now queries and merges both collections (tagging each candidate with its source collection so the final cancel-write and lookups target the right one; shifts docs derive a senior name from `careRecipients[0].name` since they have no top-level 'seniorName` field), and a new `onShiftUpdated` trigger (same file as `onAppointmentUpdated`, exported via the existing `export * from './triggers/appointmentUpdated'` wildcard in index.ts) watches 'shifts/{shiftId}` for the identical cancellation signal and reuses `handleCaregiverCancellation` unchanged — scoped to JUST the cancellation branch (arrival/confirmed/completed notifications for shifts-based visits are a separate, not-yet-built item, see below). The downstream "family picks a replacement" confirmation path (`taskApprovalHandler.ts`'s `pendingTaskConfirm` handling) already books the replacement through `createBookingTask`/`executeBookings` — already shifts-safe from earlier in this session, no change needed there.
  **Named follow-on, explicitly not fixed**: `routeCaregiver.ts`'s `handleShiftConfirmation` (fires when a caregiver declines their day-before/30-min shift-confirmation reminder) also only loads from `appointments` — but it's only ever reachable via those reminder jobs, which are themselves still appointments-only (the ~21-file scheduled-jobs gap named in the entry below), so it inherits that same fix whenever that batch gets done; not a new, independent gap.
  New tests: `caregiverCancelShiftHandler.test.ts` extended (+3: shifts-only listing, appointments+shifts merge sorted by date, shifts-doc cancellation write); new `triggers/__tests__/onShiftUpdated.test.ts` (5 tests: ignores no-status-change/non-cancellation/no-clientId, detects a real shifts cancellation and hands off to the shared replacement flow, detects the `caregiver_called_out` status variant). Full suite green throughout (`npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run`: 282 files, 3435 passed, 8 skipped).

- **2026-08-30 (request_booking now links back to a job/interview, matching handleSendBooking's job_applications/booking_requests linkage — CODE COMPLETE, full suite green, NOT yet deployed)** — Follow-up to the booking-pipeline entry directly below: re-checking the Care Requests page against this session's new `manage_booking`/`resend_booking` work surfaced that `request_booking` (the tool that actually commits a booking) was missing something `PostsPage.tsx`'s `handleSendBooking` does for a fresh (non-resend) booking sent right after an interview — it stamps `jobId`/`jobTitle`/`interviewId` onto the `booking_requests` doc AND flips the caregiver's `job_applications` doc to 'status:'accepted'`. Evia's `request_booking` created a fine booking but never did either, so a booking made via text right after an interview lost that linkage entirely. Fixed: `request_booking`'s tool schema gained an optional `interviewId` param; when passed, the case handler resolves `video_interviews/{interviewId}` → `applicationId` → `job_applications/{applicationId}.jobId` → `job_posts/{jobId}.title`, fails soft (never blocks the booking) if any lookup misses or the interview doesn't belong to this client/caregiver pair, and passes `jobId`/`jobTitle`/`interviewId`/`applicationId` through `createBookingTask` → `BookingTask` → `executeBookings`. `executeBookings` now conditionally stamps those three fields onto the `booking_requests` doc it writes, and — matching the website's immediate side effect (the application is marked accepted the moment the booking is SENT, not once the caregiver later confirms the visit) — updates `job_applications/{applicationId}` to 'status:'accepted', acceptedAt` right after the `booking_requests` write, fire-and-forget. `qaAgent.ts` updated (both the `request_booking` and 'submit_interview_feedback` lines) to tell Evia to pass `interviewId` when booking follows a "strong" interview outcome. New tests in `booking.test.ts` (3 cases: successful linkage resolution, an interview that doesn't match this client/caregiver books unlinked instead of erroring, and a booking with no `interviewId` at all stays unlinked as before). Full functions suite green (`npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run`: 281 files, 3427 passed, 8 skipped).

- **2026-08-30 (page-by-page site/Evia parity audit resumed from scratch; scheduled-jobs stale-data sweep; CODE COMPLETE for Browse Caregivers page + 3 onboarding-nudge fixes, NOT yet deployed)** — Resumed the client-side parity audit from the very beginning with a slower, page-by-page methodology (user shares a screenshot of one live site page; trace exactly what it reads/writes in Firestore; check Evia's tools against the same collections; fix gaps; verify with tests before moving on). Standing checklist per page (added to mid-session, retroactively confirmed satisfied for pages already done): (1) Evia writes to the same collections/fields the site writes to, (2) Evia checks live data before answering/acting rather than trusting memory or a stale session field, (3) every site action has a working Evia equivalent, (4) Evia matches the site's own sequencing/timing rules, (5) **new** — any scheduled or Firestore-triggered function tied to that page's collections gets checked the same way. **Browse Caregivers page (`components/FindCaregivers.tsx`) — 4 fixes, CODE COMPLETE, not yet deployed:** (a) 'schedule_interview` (mcp/server.ts) wrote 'status:'scheduled'` and proactively generated + delivered the Google Meet link and scheduled the 1-hour reminder immediately at creation — before the caregiver had replied at all; live-verified against the website's own flow, which withholds the link/reminder until the caregiver explicitly accepts. Fixed by writing 'status:'requested'` instead (matching the website's own not-yet-agreed status) and removing the premature link generation entirely — reuses the *existing* `onVideoInterviewLinkEnsure` trigger's "requested" branch (plain "please respond" text) and its "confirmed" agreed-state branch (link+reminders), rather than duplicating that logic; `respond_to_interview_request` already existed as the caregiver's SMS accept/decline path into "confirmed", so no new caregiver-side tool was needed. (b) `getCaregiverPreviewAction.ts`'s two non-scored lookup paths (exact-city, widened-city) silently ignored `needsTransportation` — only the scored (lat/lng) path applied it; added a `hasValidTransportDocs` Firestore filter (precomputed boolean already on `publicCaregiverProfiles` from earlier work) to all three. (c) 'save_caregiver_favorite` now also writes the same `users/{uid}/match_history/{caregiverId}` match-feedback signal 'services/matchFeedback.ts`'s `logMatchSignal('favorited')` writes on the website — was previously matching the 'savedCaregiverIds` write but silently skipping this secondary signal. (d) Message-write shape (Evia's `chatThread.ts` vs. the website's `chatService.ts`) verified field-for-field identical — no change needed, already fixed in an earlier pass. Deliberately deferred (not a bug, a scoping decision): the page's "Active Booking" badge reads `booking_requests`, which Evia's own booking pipeline (`agent_tasks`→'shift_offers`→`appointments`) never writes to at all — full architectural design worked out (see below) but intentionally held for whichever page turns out to own the real "send booking" action. **Booking-pipeline unification — designed, not yet built:** traced the real end-to-end website booking flow (client posts a job → caregiver applies → interview → client sends a booking via `PostsPage.tsx`'s `handleSendBooking` → `booking_requests.add()` → **caregiver clicks Accept** → `onBookingAccepted` trigger in 'shiftGenerator.ts` auto-generates 'shifts` docs going forward on a rolling 2-week basis) and confirmed Evia's pipeline is 100% structurally separate end-to-end (`agent_tasks`→`appointments`→'shift_offers`, never touching `booking_requests` or 'shifts`) — meaning every caregiver-facing website screen (`CaregiverCalendarPage`, `CaregiverBookingsPage`, `CaregiverHomeDashboard`, `CaregiverPaymentsPage` — confirmed via grep, all read exclusively from 'shifts`) is completely blind to a booking made through Evia; a caregiver has to text Evia back to interact with it at all. Confirmed the fix is NOT a straight mirror-into-`booking_requests` (its schema assumes a standing weekly `dayShiftTimes` pattern; Evia's `request_booking` tool genuinely accepts arbitrary, potentially irregular date lists — confirmed via `buildBookingQuote`'s raw `dates: string[]` input, no weekly-pattern constraint). Agreed design: the moment Evia's caregiver confirms via SMS, write one real `booking_requests` doc at 'status:'accepted'`, and write the exact matching 'shifts` docs directly (bypassing the site's day-of-week generator, which can't safely represent an irregular date list) so every caregiver-facing site screen sees it identically to a website booking; `appointments` becomes purely Evia's own in-flight negotiation state, gone once confirmed. Deliberately not started — flagged as needing its own dedicated page-trace pass (touches booking negotiation, conflict-checking, and the shift-hours billing tools) rather than being built from memory of the design discussion. **Scheduled/triggered-function stale-data sweep** (prompted by a live bug: Evia proactively texted a client "you're almost there, just tell us who needs the care" — a question already answered via the website days earlier): audited essentially every scheduled function in the codebase (~55 files in `functions/src/scheduled/` plus 9 cron jobs found living outside that folder entirely, e.g. `triggers/triggerEngine.ts`, 'shiftHours.ts`'s auto-approve/retry jobs) for the same failure shape — an autonomous message/action asserting a fact from a tracked field that the website's independent writes could have silently made false. ~50 files confirmed clean (they all re-query the live, shared collection — `appointments`, 'shiftHours`, `job_applications` — at send time rather than trusting a separately-tracked flag). **3 confirmed bugs, fixed, CODE COMPLETE, not yet deployed:** 'staleSessionNudge.ts` and `onboardingReengagement.ts` (near-identical bug: both decided someone was "still mid-onboarding" purely from `agent_sessions.onboardingStep`, with zero check of whether the client had actually finished via the website) — both now call `userHasRealOnboardingProgress`/self-heal `onboardingStep:'complete'` on the session doc when the live `users/{uid}` doc shows real progress (`jobPostingCompleted`/'seniorId`/'seniorIds`), scoped to `userType !== 'caregiver'` only since caregiver onboarding is SMS-only per CLAUDE.md (no independent website path to race against). `locationRequestNudge.ts` — its `pendingLocationRequest.nudgeSent` marker is, per its own code comment, only ever cleared by an SMS reply; added a live check of `users/{uid}.zipCode/zip/city` before nudging, clearing the marker instead of nudging if the website already captured an address. New test files: `onboardingReengagement.test.ts`, `locationRequestNudge.test.ts`; extended 'staleSessionNudge.test.ts` (added a `users` store to its existing Firestore mock). One more finding flagged but explicitly NOT fixed yet, needs more digging before it's even confirmed: `triggerEngine.ts` composes an appointment-reminder message once, at scheduling time, then fires it verbatim later — correctness depends on every possible cancel/reschedule path remembering to call `cancelTriggersByRef`, which wasn't exhaustively verified. `paywallWinback.ts` also flagged (its subscription check is fine — live, authoritative field — but it separately asserts a specific caregiver's continued availability from a snapshot that can be up to 7 days stale) — not yet fixed, not yet decided. **Care Requests page (`components/client/PostsPage.tsx`, /client/posts)** — fully traced (2411 lines; reads/writes for New Request, Edit/Cancel post, the Interviews tab, the applicants panel, and the real send-booking/hire flow, verbatim `bookingData` shape captured), then 4 fixes built, CODE COMPLETE, not yet deployed: **(a) `respond_to_job_application`'s "accept" completely rebuilt.** It used to jump straight to `job_posts.status:'filled'` + reject every other applicant — copying `hooks/useJobApplications.ts`'s `acceptApplication`, confirmed via a full-codebase grep to be DEAD CODE never called from any live component. The website has NO direct "accept an applicant" action at all — the only real path from applied to hired is schedule an interview → interview completes → the client sends a booking. Fixed: "accept" now requires `preferredDate`/`preferredTime` and creates a real interview request (delegates to the same logic 'schedule_interview` uses, extracted into a shared `createVideoInterviewRequestForTool` helper); `job_posts`/other applications are left untouched, matching the site. **(b) 'schedule_interview`'s own `job_applications` write fixed too** — it was setting 'status:'interview_scheduled'`, a value that exists nowhere on the website; since the Applicants panel's own query filters on 'status=='pending'`, this made an applicant silently vanish from the client's website panel the moment Evia scheduled an interview with them. Now only links `interviewId`, leaving status untouched. **(c) Added `complete_interview`** (there was no Evia equivalent at all for the website's "Mark as Completed" button) and **extended 'submit_interview_feedback`** to fully match the website's "Not Selected"/hire flow: `fitLevel:'no'` now sets `video_interviews.status:'declined'` + `declinedBy:'client'` and writes a `hire_decisions` record (previously did neither); `fitLevel:'strong'` now also writes `hire_decisions` alongside the existing `hire_requests` write; any decision auto-marks the interview `completed` first (one conversational turn standing in for the website's two separate button clicks). Confirmed `hire_decisions` itself has zero readers anywhere in the codebase (dead collection on the website's own side too) — written anyway for literal parity, costs nothing. **(d) `create_job_post` ("New Request") given the same zip-code treatment as every other client-facing location field this session** — it previously only collected a free-text `city` (no zip at all); now requires a 5-digit `zipCode` and auto-derives city/state/lat/lng via the same `lookupZipPlace` helper onboarding uses, plus added the previously-missing 'streetAddress`/`endDate` fields to both the tool and the shared `buildWebJobPostDoc` contract. `qaAgent.ts`'s tool-documentation prompt updated to match all of the above, including fixing a stale claim that 'schedule_interview` "texts the caregiver the Google Meet link automatically" (untrue since the earlier Browse-Caregivers-page fix). New test files: `interviewOutcomes.test.ts`, `createJobPost.test.ts`; `jobPosts.test.ts` rewritten for the new accept behavior. Two incidental findings surfaced, not yet actioned: `dbService.createJobPost`'s (website-side) field whitelist silently drops `caregiversNeeded` at post-creation time (defaults to 1 everywhere until a separate edit sets it — a website-side bug, not an Evia gap); and interview scheduling from the applicants panel is the one write on that entire page that goes through a server callable instead of a direct client-SDK write. Still not built, still the natural home for the booking-pipeline design above: the actual Send Booking/Hire flow and Resend/Cancel booking. Typecheck clean and full functions suite green throughout every fix in this entry (373 files transpiled/0 errors; ~3390 tests, a few flaky resource-contention timeouts confirmed to pass in isolation, unrelated to any of this session's changes). **OWED**: commit + push + deploy everything above (all of it is currently local, uncommitted); then build the Send Booking/Hire flow as the booking-pipeline design's first real implementation.

- **2026-08-22 (later evening — real chat-thread parity for caregiver↔family messaging, and a genuine Care Plan confirmation step before caregivers/membership; CODE COMPLETE, deploying)** — Two more parity gaps found while walking the client "Find Caregivers" page (`FindCaregivers.tsx`) against Evia's tools section by section. **(1) Messaging was one-way**: the website's "Message" button (`openChat()`) creates a real, persisted, two-way `chatRooms/{sortedIds}/messages` thread both parties can see and reply in. Evia's 'send_caregiver_message`/'send_client_message` tools only fired a one-off SMS via `trySend()` — no thread, so a caregiver's reply never surfaced back to the family the way a real chat reply would. Fixed by adding `functions/src/utils/chatThread.ts` (`relayIntoSharedChatThread`, `chatRoomIdFor`) mirroring 'services/chatService.ts`'s exact write shape (same deterministic room id, same message fields), wired into both tools so they now deliver by SMS *and* post into the same thread the website's Inbox reads from — fail-soft, a thread-write hiccup never blocks the SMS relay. **(2) No Care Plan confirmation before caregivers/membership**: live-tested and found Evia jumps straight from collection to showing caregivers with no equivalent of the website's "Looks good" review step, which the site does *before* Identity/Membership. Root cause: `continueAfterClientCollection` called `handleClientShowCaregivers` directly; the actual confirmation handler (`handleClientConfirmIntake`, `client_confirm_intake` step) already existed, fully wired in the routing table, just never reached from the modern loop-driven flow (dead code from before the loop-only rewrite). Rewired `continueAfterClientCollection` to send the intake summary and park at `client_confirm_intake` instead; `carePlanReviewedAt` (same field the website's "Looks good" button sets) now stamps inside `persistClientCareRecords`, which only runs after explicit confirmation. Updated the onboarding directive's completion message so it no longer promises "pulling up caregivers now" the instant collection ends (would have contradicted the new confirmation step). While re-wiring, found and fixed two more staleness bugs in the confirmation handler itself: `buildIntakeSummary` and `extractIntakeCorrections` still only read/extracted legacy field names (`daysPerWeek`, `budget.min/max`) instead of the current loop fields ('selectedDays`, `rate`) — silently dropping schedule and rate from both the summary and any correction attempt; also added `relationship` to the extractable-correction fields, canonicalized through the same `parent`/'spouse`/`other` mapping 'save_onboarding_field` uses. Full suites green (functions: 270 files/3311 tests; root unaffected — no frontend files touched). **OWED**: commit + push + deploy, then live re-test of both — confirm a caregiver's reply now shows up in the family's website Inbox, and confirm the new intake-summary confirmation turn appears before caregivers/membership on a fresh conversation.

- **2026-08-22 (evening — 4 live-testing fixes on top of the caregiver-matching deploy: address zip/city, membership-contradiction recurrence, relationship value, same-address confirmation; CODE COMPLETE, deploying)** — Live-tested the caregiver-matching deploy above and found 4 more concrete bugs, all traced to exact code, fixed, and test-locked. **(1) Address zip/city**: a client typed "4746 Campbell Ave" (street only) and Evia never asked for city or zip — `zipCode` was only in `CLIENT_ALLOWED_FIELDS`, not required, so collection could (and did) complete without it. Verified against the website wizard (`ClientJobPostingWizard.tsx`'s `handleHomeZip`/`handleCustomZip`): it REQUIRES a 5-digit zip and auto-derives city/state via `api.zippopotam.us`, never lets the family type a city at all. Matched that exactly: added `lookupZipPlace` to `functions/src/utils/geocode.ts` (same zippopotam.us call, now also returns city/state, not just lat/lng); made `zipCode` required in `CLIENT_REQUIRED_FIELDS`; wired auto-derivation into 'save_onboarding_field` (mcp/server.ts) so saving a zip immediately and automatically saves city/state too — the model is never asked or trusted to extract a city from free text again. **(2) Membership/matching contradiction recurred** (same class as a 2026-08-22-morning fix, different code path): right after the LAST required field (`rate`) was answered, Evia's own free-form closing reply (composed when calling `complete_collection`) said "next step is membership payment, and once that's active I can move you into matching" — then immediately sent a real caregiver preview in the next message, a visible self-contradiction. The morning fix only guarded `handleClientPresentPlan`'s message; this closing reply is generated by the model itself in response to the `complete_collection` tool result, a different, unguarded spot. Fixed in two places for reinforcement: the `complete_collection` tool's own description in `mcp/server.ts` now explicitly forbids the framing, and `onboardingDirective.ts`'s completion-instruction names the exact wrong phrasing as a concrete negative example. **(3) Relationship value**: a client said "it's for my parent" and Evia saved `relationship: "child"` — not wrong data (technically your relation to a parent is "child"), but not the website's canonical enum either. Verified against the wizard (`ClientJobPostingWizard.tsx` step 9): the ONLY 4 values are `myself`/`parent`/'spouse`/`other` — no daughter/son/child at all — and `CarePlan.tsx`'s duplicate-recipient guard does an exact `.toLowerCase() === 'myself'` check, so any other value silently breaks it. Complication found mid-fix: Evia already uses `relationship: "self"` as its OWN internal sentinel in 6+ places (`onboardingSteps.client.ts`, `qaAgent.ts`, `careRecipients.ts`, locked in by `whoIsWhoCoverage.test.ts` asserting the literal string) for self-referential voice/logic — completely different value space than the website's `"myself"`. Fixed correctly at the boundary instead of renaming Evia's internal sentinel: added `toWebsiteRelationship()` (`careRecipients.ts`) translating `"self"` → `"myself"` ONLY at the point data reaches a website-facing document (`job_postings`, `carePlans.recipientPlans` via `allCareRecipients`, `clientIntakes`, 'senior_profiles`) — Evia's internal `onboardingData.relationship` keeps using `"self"` everywhere else, untouched. Also added a code-side canonicalization backstop in `normalizeOnboardingFieldValue` (`onboardingContract.ts`) mapping daughter/son/child/mother/mom/father/dad → `parent` and wife/husband/partner → 'spouse`, with anything unrecognized defaulting to `other` rather than passing through raw (deliberately different from the existing jobType-canonicalization policy of passing unknowns through, since `other` is a real, intentional bucket in this specific small closed enum). **(4) Same-address confirmation never asked**: no field ever tracked whether "is care at the same address as home?" was actually asked — the model could (and did) silently assume same-address and mirror home fields into the care fields with nothing to catch a skip, unlike required DATA fields which are checked before completion. Made it a real required field: added `homeZipCode` (required, same auto-derive-city/state treatment as care `zipCode`) and 'sameAsHomeAddress` (required boolean) to `CLIENT_REQUIRED_FIELDS`; answering `true` auto-mirrors `homeStreet/homeCity/homeZipCode/homeState` into 'street/city/zipCode/state` inside 'save_onboarding_field`, matching the wizard's own copy-on-same-address behavior. Full suites green throughout all 4 fixes (functions: 270 files/3304 tests; root: 293 files/3682 tests). Also surfaced but explicitly out of scope for this pass, per user: the "should every field be hard-required" question — concluded no, only the address belongs there (matches the site's own required treatment); true optional items (photo, pets, payment method) stay declinable, but should eventually get an explicit "asked-and-declined" marker instead of relying on the model's memory of its own conversation, a different and smaller follow-up than blanket-requiring everything. **OWED**: commit + push + deploy (in progress), then live SMS re-test of all 4 fixes with a genuinely fresh conversation (the earlier test conversation already completed under the old rules and won't retroactively gain a zip/coordinates).

- **2026-08-22 (later same day — caregiver-matching quality port, `daysFlexible` fix, and full removal of the post-payment care-plan interview; CODE COMPLETE, deploying)** — Three pieces, same session as the phone role-lock fix above. **(1) Caregiver-matching port**: Evia's SMS caregiver preview (`get_caregiver_preview`) used to do only an exact-city-string match with a crude "widen to everyone" fallback — no distance, skills, or availability scoring at all, unlike the website dashboard's real "Nearby Caregivers" widget (`hooks/useNearbyCaregiversWithScores.ts` — haversine distance, skills-overlap, availability-overlap, service-radius + transport-doc hard filters). Ported that scoring logic into a new backend module `functions/src/agents/caregiverMatchScoring.ts` (pure, unit-tested functions: `haversineDistanceMiles`, 'skillsOverlap`, `availabilityOverlap`, `hasValidTransportDocs`, 'scoreAndRankCaregivers`). `getCaregiverPreviewAction.ts` now takes optional `lat`/`lng`/'schedule`/`needsTransportation`; when present, runs a primary pass (25mi + service radius + transport requirement) and, if nobody qualifies, a relaxed backup pass over the same candidate pool (distance/radius filters dropped, but the transportation requirement is ALWAYS still enforced — it's a real capability need, not a soft preference) — falls back to the old exact-city-match when no coordinates are given, so existing callers/tests are unaffected. Closed a real underlying gap: SMS clients never had real coordinates stored anywhere (unlike caregivers, who get geocoded via `ensureCaregiverCoords`) — added a client-side twin `ensureClientCoords` (same free zippopotam.us/Nominatim geocoding, no API key, negligible cost) wired into `handleClientShowCaregivers` before both the care-plan write and the caregiver preview, so `carePlans.locationPool` and the new scoring both get real coordinates for the first time. Verified this also fixes the website's own "Nearby Caregivers" dashboard widget and "Find Caregivers" page for SMS-onboarded clients (both read live from the same Firestore fields Evia now populates) — not live-tested end-to-end yet, but confirmed at the code level. **(2) `daysFlexible` fix**: confirmed via code that this field (part of wizard step 6, "days flexible" toggle) was defined in the wizard-parity contract module but never added to `CLIENT_ALLOWED_FIELDS` — 'save_onboarding_field` silently rejected it even though the directive asks about it. One-line fix. **(3) Care-plan interview removed entirely** (not just trimmed): user asked whether the post-payment interview (`carePlanInterview.ts` — task detail, medications, daily routine, doctor contacts, emergency contact) was needed at all, since it does things the website's own wizard/pages don't do. Investigation found: the wizard's `careNeeds` step is flat, 8 broad categories, no sub-task detail — so the interview's task-detail collection looked like real added value beyond the site at first. But further verification found `components/CarePlan.tsx` AND `components/client/postJob/Step3CareNeeds.tsx` already have a live, working sub-category checklist UI writing to the exact same `carePlans.recipientPlans.{key}.careNeedDetails` field the interview's 'save_care_task_detail` tool also wrote to — so even the non-medical task-detail piece was fully redundant with an existing site feature, confirming the user's instinct. Removed: `carePlanInterview.ts` + its test, the `carePlanInterviewBackfill` migration, the 'save_care_task_detail` MCP tool, and the `medications`/`diagnoses`/`dailyRoutine`/`dietaryRestrictions`/`dietaryNotes`/'specialInstructions`/`doctorContacts`/`medicationsConfirmedNone`/`emergencyContactDeclined` fields from `update_care_plan` (kept `careNeeds`/`notes`/`emergencyContacts`/`accessCodes`, and simplified away the now-dead multi-recipient medical-field scoping block in its handler). Removed all wiring: `qaAgent.ts` (interview directive injection, tool-availability guarantee, completion check — plus a stale leftover in `buildClientCoreContext`'s live-context builder that was still listing the removed fields as "on file"), `routeIntent.ts` (quick-reply-bypass gate), `permissionsConversation.ts` (both kickoff call sites), `pendingActions.ts` (risk-gating field lists), `toolCapabilities.ts`, `launchActionParity.ts`, `carePlanHistory.ts` (version-history change-summary field list). One honest tradeoff accepted: the job-post-enrichment + engaged-caregiver-follow-up automation that used to fire on interview completion has no equivalent trigger tied to the website's own `careNeedDetails` editing — that automation is simply gone now, since it was never an advertised site feature to begin with, just something Evia bolted on. Full suites green throughout all three pieces (functions: 270 files/3294 tests; root: 293 files/3672 tests; frontend `vite build` clean). **Also surfaced, deferred to a "pre-launch checklist" memory** ([[project_evia_live_data_and_scope_plan]]): the broader "Evia hard-declines anything outside what the site offers" instruction is still not built (today only removed the one concrete contradiction); the site's real feature list was found to be broader than a prior memory assumed (missing Messages/Inbox and Help/live-support-chat entirely), and the user correctly noted scope shouldn't be limited to nav tabs anyway — real backend processes (membership billing, background checks, Stripe Connect payouts, interviews, shift execution) are legitimately in-scope even without a dedicated nav item. **OWED**: commit + push + deploy (in progress), then live SMS test of the caregiver-preview scoring and confirm the care-plan-interview removal doesn't leave any dangling references live.

- **2026-08-22 (phone locked to first onboarding role — client/caregiver confusion fix; DEPLOYED-VERIFIED)** — Live SMS testing (both roles tested on one phone) left the account "confused" about whether it was a client or a caregiver. Investigated every account-creation entry point (website OTP signup, cold-SMS caregiver signup, all `.where("phone","==",...)` lookups) before touching code: the website path (`createWebOnboardingSession` in `functions/src/index.ts`, `ensureWebAccount` in `onboardingConversation.ts`) already guards `userType` correctly (`if (!userData.userType)` — never overwrites an existing role), and Firebase Phone Auth returns the same uid for a given phone every time, so two separate `users` docs for one phone was never actually the mechanism. The real bug was a silent role-flip at exactly one write site: the caregiver's 'stripe_connect` finalize step (`onboardingConversation.ts`, `users/{authUid}.set({userType: "caregiver", ...}, {merge:true})`) had no guard at all — the one unconditional exception to the "never overwrite an existing role" rule used everywhere else. Fixed two layers: (1) that write now checks for an existing client account with real progress ('seniorId`/'seniorIds`) first and refuses to overwrite if found, logging the conflict instead; (2) `handleAskRole` (the very first SMS question — "are you looking for care or are you a caregiver?") now calls a new `findConflictingRoleAccount(phone, claimedRole)` check before proceeding, so a conflicting phone gets told which role it's already registered under immediately, instead of going through an entire signup only to hit the wall at the end. Full suites green (functions: 270 files/3306 tests; root: 293 files/3684 tests; 0 failed). Committed `784a030`, pushed, deployed and verified live via `gcloud` (`v1-linqWebhook` updateTime `2026-08-22T09:54:01Z`). Explicitly scoped out at user's direction: no extra user-facing message on the website OTP screen itself when someone picks the "wrong" role tab for an already-registered phone — it just silently (and correctly) routes them into their real existing role.

- **2026-08-22 (4 bugs found live-testing the SMS wizard-parity work from the day before; CODE COMPLETE)** — User live-tested the deployed SMS flow and found 4 concrete bugs, each traced to an exact cause: (1) `handleClientConfirmName`'s "corrected"/"confirmed" branches (`onboardingConversation.ts`) had a hardcoded message asking about relationship/senior-name immediately after name-confirm — a *different* code path than the directive fixed the day before, so the strict question order never actually took effect for the first question; changed to ask care frequency (wizard step 1) instead. (2) `complete_collection` only gates on the 12 *required* fields, and the instruction said to fire it the instant that list is empty — so photo/pets+smoking/`caregiversNeeded`/`ongoing`/`endDate`/`jobDescription` (all optional, not required) got silently skipped whenever a required field like `rate` happened to be answered last; fixed by splitting every previously-bundled optional item into its own numbered step in `onboardingDirective.ts`, adding an `OPTIONAL_ORDER_ITEMS` check that inserts a "you haven't brought these up yet" gate line before allowing `complete_collection` even after required fields are done (prompt-only — no new field-tracking plumbing, matches the existing "age is optional, ask once" pattern). (3) `handleClientPresentPlan`'s LLM-generated membership pitch was self-contradicting live ("next step is membership, once that's active I can move you into matching" — right after a real caregiver preview was already shown) — added an explicit instruction forbidding that framing, reframing membership as what lets the family message/book the caregiver just shown. (4) `jobPostingCompleted` was stamped inside `buildAndSaveJobPost` (post-payment) — live-confirmed wrong: logging into the website right after intake-confirm (data already visible in `carePlans`/`job_postings`) still showed the `ClientJobPostingWizard` overlay, because on the *website* the wizard's Submit sets this flag *before* Identity/Membership, not after. Moved the stamp into `persistClientCareRecords` (fires at intake-confirm, the true equivalent moment); left `carePlanReviewedAt` where it was (post-payment confirm) since that one's correctly decoupled — a wizard-only web client also never gets it set without separately visiting the Care Plan page. Also confirmed live: `get_caregiver_preview` (shows a real, approved caregiver as a pre-payment teaser, same visibility rules as `FindCaregivers`) is a real, intentional feature, not a bug. Full suite green (293 files / 3684 tests / 8 skipped / 0 failed) after all 4 fixes. **OWED**: commit + deploy, then re-test live.

- **2026-08-21 (client SMS onboarding matched to web wizard — Care Plan/job-posting domain; CODE COMPLETE, DEPLOYING)** — Live walkthrough session (user drove the web wizard screen-by-screen while every write was traced, then compared against a live SMS conversation with Evia) found the SMS side wrote to the right collection *names* but a different *shape* than `createJobPosting` ('services/api.ts`) — different field names (`hourlyRate`/`description` vs `rate`/`jobDescription`), nested `location{}`/'schedule{}` vs the wizard's flat fields, missing fields entirely (`rateFlexible`, `photoURL`, `caregiversNeeded`, `daysFlexible`), and question order that only followed the wizard "roughly" (live-tested: Evia jumped to relationship as question #2, skipping frequency/address/schedule). Closed 3 gaps the wizard has that Evia never asked about: care-recipient photo (new `handleInboundClientRecipientPhoto` media handler), `caregiversNeeded` (1–4, default 1), `ongoing`/`endDate`. Removed the incremental per-field mirror to `job_postings`/`carePlans`/'senior_profiles` (`mcp/server.ts`'s 'save_onboarding_field` used to write them continuously during collection) — now writes nothing to those collections until finalization, matching the wizard's "nothing until Submit." Confirmed (no build needed) Evia already has an explicit "here's your summary, reply YES to post it" confirmation gate (`presentPrefilledJobPostSummary`/`handleJobConfirmPrefill`) — the new `carePlans.carePlanReviewedAt` stamp (closes a shared bug: never set by anyone, on either channel, so Care Plan could never show 100% on the dashboard) rides on that same existing confirmation rather than auto-stamping, matching the website's explicit "Looks good" button semantics. Confirmed Identity Check and Membership were already unified before this session (Evia texts the same Stripe-hosted links, same webhooks write the same fields) — no fix needed there. Full suite green throughout (293 files / 3684 tests / 8 skipped / 0 failed). **Explicitly deferred, not fixed**: the wizard's 2 cosmetic transition screens (zero data, user said skip); a general "Evia checks live Firestore data, not cached session/memory, whenever a conversation touches on something" capability (user wants this next — investigated this session: care-plan medications/account-status/appointments are already re-read live every turn via `buildClientCoreContext`, but the `job_postings`/`carePlans` address fields built this session have zero live-read mechanism anywhere); a 3-way decision on medication/doctor-contact/diagnosis handling in the separate post-payment care-plan interview (user flagged surprise this existed — it's a real 2026-07-15 founder decision with real privacy engineering already, not scope creep, but user wants to revisit; see memory `project_carePlanInterview_scope_revisit`). **OWED**: commit + deploy (in progress), then live SMS test.
